**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, interactive_branch, mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, side_by_side.

#### EDU022 The Four-Bit Lantern

A technician must display thirteen using four binary lamps, but initially reads 1101 as ordinary decimal digits. Labeling lamp weights eight, four, two, and one makes the lit total clear.

**Hook:** Four lamps, thirteen signals: how?

**Visible action:** Four weighted lamp blocks toggle; illuminated weights fall into an addition tray showing thirteen.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Binary place value uses powers of two.

**Gate:** Use unsigned four-bit integers and avoid implying this explains all computer number formats.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** serial_challenge, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway, silent_visual.

#### EDU023 The Parcel Queue Remembers

A dispatcher must sort parcels by destination while preserving arrival order within each destination, but an unstable reshuffle swaps two customers. Numbered tags make the mistake visible and a stable sort preserves both requirements.

**Hook:** These parcels match. Which one should leave first?

**Visible action:** Colored parcels sort into destination lanes while tiny arrival-number badges remain attached and comparable.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Stable sorting preserves the prior order of equal-key items.

**Gate:** State the sort key and tie-order requirement; do not claim stability is always necessary.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, behind_the_scenes, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, mechanism_cutaway.

#### EDU024 The Crowded Hash Locker

A librarian wants fast storage lookup, but two book keys map to the same locker. A visible collision list retains both books and a full-key check retrieves the requested one.

**Hook:** Two keys, one locker: did a book disappear?

**Visible action:** Key cards pass through a toy modulo machine; colliding cards stack in one bucket and are compared individually.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Hash collisions require an explicit resolution and key-checking strategy.

**Gate:** Use a toy hash table; do not conflate indexing hashes with cryptographic security.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, interactive_branch, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU025 The Double-Counted Visitor

A curator wants a visitor total from two entrance logs, but counts a returning badge twice. Matching a defined visit identifier and showing the excluded duplicate produces a transparent total.

**Hook:** Is this a new visitor, or the same badge again?

**Visible action:** Two record streams merge; matching visit-ID cards align, and duplicate records move into an audit tray.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Deduplication depends on a clearly defined identity and counting rule.

**Gate:** Use invented records; explain that people, visits, and devices are different counting units.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, behind_the_scenes, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, mechanism_cutaway.

#### EDU026 The Two Endings Merge

Two animators must combine edits to one story, but both changed the same final line. A branch diagram preserves their versions and highlights the conflict so they can choose a deliberate combined ending.

**Hook:** Which ending did we actually agree to keep?

**Visible action:** A timeline forks into two colored branches; conflicting text blocks meet at a highlighted merge checkpoint.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Version control preserves change history; conflicting intent still needs a decision.

**Gate:** Use invented text and a generic workflow; exact platform commands require separate review.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway, behind_the_scenes, interactive_branch, step_by_step.

#### EDU027 The Stripe Compressor Meets Confetti

A messenger wants to shrink an image description, but a repeated-run code that helps stripes grows bulky on alternating pixels. Comparing encoded lengths reveals when this particular compression scheme helps.

**Hook:** Can the same shortcut make one picture bigger?

**Visible action:** Long same-color strips become count-color tokens; alternating pixels produce many small tokens and a longer ruler.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Compression effectiveness depends on both the data and the encoding overhead.

**Gate:** Specify token costs and a reversible toy encoding; avoid claims about all compression.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, prediction_quiz, myth_check, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step.

#### EDU028 The Disappearing Checkerboard

A camera robot wants to capture fine stripes, but its sparse sample points miss alternating details. Sliding the sampling grid changes the recorded pattern and exposes the ambiguity.

**Hook:** Why do the stripes vanish when the camera steps back?

**Visible action:** A sampling lattice moves over a striped panel; selected points build a coarse image beside the original.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Sampling can lose detail or create misleading patterns when resolution is insufficient.

**Gate:** Define the synthetic pattern and sampler; real-camera optics need additional source review.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU029 The Last Search Arrives First

A search box must show results for the newest query, but a slower old request arrives after a newer one and overwrites it. Request identifiers let the display ignore stale responses.

**Hook:** Why did the old answer replace the new one?

**Visible action:** Two query envelopes race along unequal tracks; a current-request gate rejects the late obsolete envelope.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Asynchronous completion order may differ from request order.

**Gate:** Use a toy interface; actual cancellation, errors, and server behavior vary.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway, interactive_branch, side_by_side, prediction_quiz.

#### EDU030 The Recommendation Echo Room

A curator wants a varied animation playlist, but a toy recommender only learns from the clips it already chose to show. An exploration lane exposes unseen options and reveals the feedback loop.

**Hook:** What can this system learn from a choice it never offered?

**Visible action:** A recommendation carousel feeds clicks back into itself until an exploratory card enters from a separate lane.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Observed preferences can reflect what a system chose to expose.

**Gate:** Label the algorithm invented; do not imply all recommendation systems behave identically.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, myth_check, interactive_branch, side_by_side, narrative_lesson.

### Learning learning/study

#### EDU031 The Closed-Notebook Bridge

A learner wants to know whether a diagram is understood, but recognizes every label only while the page is open. Rebuilding it from blank blocks reveals gaps that can be checked against the source.

**Hook:** Can you rebuild it after the page disappears?

**Visible action:** A labeled diagram folds away; blank blocks are assembled from memory and mismatches glow during comparison.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A retrieval attempt can reveal gaps that looking at the answer hides.

**Gate:** Review learning-science claims; make no guaranteed retention or grade promises.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** prediction_quiz, step_by_step, serial_challenge, side_by_side, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU032 The Review Calendar Has Gaps

A student needs several chances to revisit a vocabulary set, but piles every review into one afternoon. A calendar animation redistributes the same planned sessions and records what needs another look.

**Hook:** What if the next review happened on another day?

**Visible action:** Equal-duration review blocks move from a crowded day into separated calendar slots; recall checks leave small flags.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A study plan can schedule repeated reviews across time and adjust from recall.

**Gate:** Review spacing evidence; no universally optimal interval or fixed learning outcome is claimed.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, behind_the_scenes, side_by_side, interactive_branch, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU033 The Example Loses Its Training Wheels

A learner wants to solve a new worked problem, but copying every displayed step hides which choices are understood. Successively removing selected steps invites the learner to supply and check them.

**Hook:** This time, one step is missing. What belongs here?

**Visible action:** A completed solution scroll replaces first one, then several blocks with empty sockets that accept learner choices.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Worked examples can become practice when support is deliberately reduced.

**Gate:** Review scaffolding claims; keep example difficulty and prerequisite knowledge explicit.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** serial_challenge, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, side_by_side, interactive_branch.

#### EDU034 The Mixed-Tool Workshop

An apprentice wants to choose the right method for each puzzle, but grouped practice lets the shelf label make every choice. Mixed puzzle cards remove that cue and require a reason before selecting a tool.

**Hook:** Which tool fits when the shelf stops telling you?

**Visible action:** Three symbolic tools face shuffled shape puzzles; a reason card must snap in before a tool can move.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Choosing a method is a separate skill from carrying out a known method.

**Gate:** Present a limited study illustration; benefits of mixed practice depend on the material.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** interactive_branch, prediction_quiz, serial_challenge, side_by_side, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU035 The Confidence Stamp Audit

A quiz maker wants to judge readiness, but stamps every familiar-looking answer as certain. Comparing confidence stamps with checked answers reveals which judgments need recalibration.

**Hook:** How sure are you before the answer flips over?

**Visible action:** Answer cards receive small confidence meters; cards flip and sort into confidence-versus-correctness bins.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Compare confidence with checked performance instead of relying on familiarity alone.

**Gate:** Use invented quiz scores and avoid labeling a learner or inferring clinical traits.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** prediction_quiz, behind_the_scenes, step_by_step, serial_challenge, side_by_side.

#### EDU036 The Museum of Useful Mistakes

A learner wants to stop repeating one algebra slip, but an answer-only notebook hides where it begins. An error exhibit pairs the mistaken step, its cause, and a corrected counterexample.

**Hook:** Which tiny step broke the whole solution?

**Visible action:** A solution strip pauses at one sign change; a magnifier extracts it into a three-panel error card.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Recording the specific error can make the next check more targeted.

**Gate:** Check all mathematics; frame mistakes as information rather than personal failure.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** behind_the_scenes, step_by_step, serial_challenge, prediction_quiz, side_by_side.

#### EDU037 The Assignment With No First Door

A student wants to start a large project, but a single giant task block has no obvious entry point. Breaking out a concrete first action opens a path to the remaining steps.

**Hook:** What can you do before you can do the whole thing?

**Visible action:** A towering assignment block unfolds into small action tiles, beginning with opening the brief and extracting one requirement.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A large goal becomes easier to initiate when its next action is explicit.

**Gate:** Offer an organizational example, not mental-health treatment or a promise against procrastination.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, step_by_step, interactive_branch, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU038 The Explain-It Puppet

A learner wants to teach a process to a puppet, but a confident explanation skips the step that connects input to output. The puppet asks what changes next, exposing the gap for source checking.

**Hook:** What happens between those two boxes?

**Visible action:** A puppet follows a block diagram until a missing connector stops it; the learner adds a verified transition.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Explaining a sequence can expose missing links in understanding.

**Gate:** Do not invent the missing explanation; any factual lesson needs its own source check.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, interactive_branch, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU039 The Note With a Broken Tether

A researcher wants to reuse a striking claim, but its note has lost the original source and surrounding context. Reattaching a citation card and checking the passage distinguishes support from speculation.

**Hook:** Where did this sentence come from?

**Visible action:** A claim block dangles from a severed line; source, date, and context cards reconnect before it enters an outline.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Useful research notes preserve a path back to supporting evidence.

**Gate:** Use invented sources or verified public ones; a citation alone does not establish truth.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** behind_the_scenes, step_by_step, narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, side_by_side.

#### EDU040 The Practice Session Has a Finish Line

A learner wants a manageable practice session, but an undefined goal keeps expanding whenever another exercise appears. Choosing a small task, a checking step, and a stopping point produces a reviewable session.

**Hook:** How will you know this practice session is done?

**Visible action:** An endless conveyor gains a gate after a selected set of exercises; a final check card unlocks the exit.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Define both the task and what completion looks like before expanding a session.

**Gate:** Do not prescribe universal attention spans or imply one routine suits everyone.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, interactive_branch, narrative_lesson, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

### Learning language/communication

#### EDU041 The Pronoun With Two Owners

A courier must deliver a note saying Alex told Sam that their bag was missing, but cannot tell whose bag it means. Replacing the ambiguous pronoun with the intended name unlocks the correct route.

**Hook:** Whose bag is actually missing?

**Visible action:** Two stick figures tug on one pronoun bubble until a named label connects it to a single bag.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An explicit noun can resolve ambiguity when a pronoun has multiple possible referents.

**Gate:** Use invented people and preserve intentional ambiguity when it serves the author.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** interactive_branch, narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, step_by_step.

#### EDU042 The Listener Sends a Mirror

A builder wants to carry out a request, but assumes that make it lighter means use a paler color. Paraphrasing the intended change reveals that the requester meant reduce weight before any work begins.

**Hook:** Lighter in color, or lighter to carry?

**Visible action:** One word forks into a paint swatch and a weighing scale; a confirming reply locks the chosen branch.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Checking meaning can prevent action on an ambiguous request.

**Gate:** Do not imply confirmation is required for every ordinary low-risk phrase.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** interactive_branch, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, narrative_lesson, step_by_step.

#### EDU043 The Comma Changes the Guest List

An event host wants to label snacks for guests, but an unpunctuated line combines categories in two possible ways. Brackets and punctuation make the intended grouping visible before the labels print.

**Hook:** How many groups did this sentence invite?

**Visible action:** A sentence splits into movable phrase blocks; alternative brackets form different groupings and matching guest icons.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Punctuation and sentence structure help readers identify intended grouping.

**Gate:** Check the chosen sentence for genuine alternative readings rather than a contrived grammar rule.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** prediction_quiz, silent_visual, side_by_side, step_by_step, interactive_branch.

#### EDU044 The Same Request Wears Two Coats

A teammate needs the same missing file from a close colleague and an unfamiliar organizer, but copies a context-heavy shorthand message to both. Adding necessary context adapts the second request without changing its purpose.

**Hook:** What does this reader already know?

**Visible action:** A compact request bubble gains file name, deadline context, and greeting blocks as its audience marker changes.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Adapt context and tone to the reader while preserving the underlying request.

**Gate:** Avoid treating one cultural register as universally correct or inventing the sender’s feelings.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, behind_the_scenes, interactive_branch, narrative_lesson, step_by_step.

#### EDU045 The Idiom Meets a Literal Painter

A translator wants to convey that a task is easy, but a literal painting of an idiom produces an irrelevant object. A meaning card bridges to plain language and, only after review, an appropriate target-language expression.

**Hook:** Should we translate the picture or the meaning?

**Visible action:** An idiom bubble becomes a literal sketch, then passes through a meaning gate before a new expression appears.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An idiom’s intended meaning may not survive word-for-word translation.

**Gate:** Have a fluent reviewer verify any actual language pair and regional usage.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** myth_check, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, narrative_lesson, step_by_step.

#### EDU046 The Directions Need a Landmark

A visitor wants to find a room, but left-right instructions change meaning when the starting orientation changes. A fixed landmark and a simple route sketch give the visitor a stable reference.

**Hook:** Left from whose point of view?

**Visible action:** A rotating stick figure follows conflicting arrows until a fixed entrance icon anchors the map.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Directions become clearer when the starting point and frame of reference are explicit.

**Gate:** Use a fictional indoor route; do not present it as accessible or emergency navigation advice.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** silent_visual, interactive_branch, narrative_lesson, side_by_side, prediction_quiz.

#### EDU047 The Interrupted Thought Queue

A meeting group wants every proposal considered, but overlapping speech bubbles bury a quiet unfinished idea. A visible speaking queue and a shared written option let the group return to each contribution.

**Hook:** Whose idea fell between the speech bubbles?

**Visible action:** Speech bubbles collide, then settle into a queue; a text card joins through a parallel written lane.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A communication process can create more than one way to contribute.

**Gate:** Do not equate silence with lack of ideas or prescribe one speaking style for everyone.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, step_by_step, interactive_branch.

#### EDU048 The Icon That Meant Two Things

A designer wants a button understood without explanation, but two viewers read the same star as favorite and rating. Pairing it with a clear label and checking the intended action resolves the prototype confusion.

**Hook:** Will this star save it, or score it?

**Visible action:** One star button forks into two predicted outcomes; a text label snaps beneath the selected action.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An icon’s meaning may need context, labeling, and user checking.

**Gate:** Do not claim symbols are universally intuitive; real-interface accessibility needs review.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** prediction_quiz, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes, interactive_branch, step_by_step.

#### EDU049 The Feedback Arrow Finds a Target

An editor wants a scene improved, but the note make it better gives the animator no usable target. Pointing to a specific confusing moment and explaining its effect creates an actionable revision request.

**Hook:** Which moment needs to change?

**Visible action:** A vague cloud shrinks into an arrow on one frame, followed by observation and desired-effect cards.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Useful feedback identifies the location, observed problem, and intended result.

**Gate:** Keep observations separate from personal judgments; do not invent consensus about quality.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** behind_the_scenes, step_by_step, side_by_side, narrative_lesson, interactive_branch.

#### EDU050 The Summary Drops Its Anchor

A reporter wants to shorten a discussion, but cuts away the condition that made a proposal acceptable. A comparison restores the condition and labels remaining disagreement instead of inventing a unanimous decision.

**Hook:** What disappeared when the meeting became one sentence?

**Visible action:** A long transcript folds into summary cards; a dangling condition tag signals a lost qualification.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A concise summary should preserve material conditions, uncertainty, and disagreement.

**Gate:** Use an invented meeting; do not attribute decisions or approval to real people.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, myth_check, behind_the_scenes, step_by_step, prediction_quiz.

### Learning work/process

#### EDU051 The Handoff With No Handle

A team must pass a finished asset to the next person, but the package contains a file with no version, owner, or next step. A handoff card supplies those details and lets work continue.

**Hook:** The file arrived. What should happen to it now?

**Visible action:** A parcel stalls between desks until version, owner, and next-action tags click onto its handle.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A handoff needs enough context for the receiver to take the next action.

**Gate:** Use invented project details and avoid implying tags replace actual access checks.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, step_by_step, side_by_side, interactive_branch, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU052 The Narrowest Gate

A production line needs more finished frames, but extra workers feed a review gate that can process only one frame at a time. Measuring the queue reveals the constraint and shifting effort there improves the toy flow.

**Hook:** Why is working faster making this pile bigger?

**Visible action:** Wide upstream conveyors dump frames before a narrow gate; counters track arrivals, departures, and queue length.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Improving a non-constraining step may only increase waiting work.

**Gate:** State the simulated arrival and service rates; real organizational bottlenecks require observation.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, step_by_step, silent_visual.

#### EDU053 The Checklist Remembers the Quiet Step

A recurring export must include subtitles, but the dramatic animation work distracts the operator from the final accessibility check. A short checklist puts the overlooked step at the point of export.

**Hook:** Which small step disappears when the big work feels done?

**Visible action:** An export lever pauses beside three checkboxes; the subtitle preview opens before the lever completes its path.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A checklist can make a repeatable, easily forgotten step visible at the right moment.

**Gate:** Keep the checklist specific; checking a box alone does not verify subtitle quality.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, behind_the_scenes, narrative_lesson, side_by_side, prediction_quiz.

#### EDU054 The Task That Waited on Tomorrow

A team wants to finish a demo Friday, but schedules the sound mix before the narration is approved. A dependency graph makes the impossible order visible and moves approval ahead of the mix.

**Hook:** Can this step start before its input exists?

**Visible action:** Task cards connect by arrows; a downstream card hits a locked gate until its predecessor turns ready.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Dependencies constrain which work can start and when.

**Gate:** Dates and durations are invented; do not imply a dependency chart predicts all delays.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, interactive_branch, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, side_by_side.

#### EDU055 The Five Almost-Finished Posters

A designer wants five completed posters, but keeps switching whenever a new idea appears. A visible limit on active drafts lets one poster cross the finish line before another enters.

**Hook:** Why are five busy canvases producing zero finished posters?

**Visible action:** Five half-painted boards compete for one brush; an active-work gate admits only the chosen small set.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Limiting simultaneous work can make completion and blocked items easier to see.

**Gate:** No universal productivity gain or ideal work-in-progress limit is asserted.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, silent_visual.

#### EDU056 The Reversible Door

A product team wants to test two layouts, but treats a replaceable mockup as if it were a permanent public launch. Marking reversibility and consequence lets them try the mockup while reserving review for the lasting decision.

**Hook:** Which door lets us walk back out?

**Visible action:** Two doors display undo paths; a sandbox preview loops back while a public-release door requires a review key.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Decision process should reflect consequences, reversibility, and actual authority.

**Gate:** Reversibility never substitutes for required permission, privacy, or safety review.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** interactive_branch, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway.

#### EDU057 The Request That Grew Extra Wheels

An animator wants to deliver a short loop, but each casual suggestion silently adds another scene. A visible scope card separates the agreed deliverable from optional additions and prompts a real tradeoff.

**Hook:** When did a loop become a whole episode?

**Visible action:** A small project cart accumulates unapproved wheels and trailers; optional cards detach into a separate review queue.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Changing scope should trigger an explicit choice about priorities, time, and approval.

**Gate:** Do not assign commitments or reject stakeholder requests on anyone’s behalf.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, behind_the_scenes, interactive_branch, side_by_side, step_by_step.

#### EDU058 The Assumption Under the Floor

A planner wants to compare two production routes, but one estimate silently assumes existing voice recordings. Pulling up the floorboard reveals the assumption and recalculating both routes makes the comparison usable.

**Hook:** What is this estimate standing on?

**Visible action:** Two timelines rest on foundation blocks; removing a missing-recording assumption lengthens only the affected path.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An estimate is easier to evaluate when its key assumptions are visible.

**Gate:** Use invented times and costs; avoid presenting the toy forecast as a real quote.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** behind_the_scenes, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, mechanism_cutaway, interactive_branch.

#### EDU059 The Definition-of-Done Turnstile

A team wants to close a task, but one person means rendered while another means reviewed and delivered. A shared set of observable completion checks aligns the label before the task exits.

**Hook:** Done in whose sense of the word?

**Visible action:** A completed frame approaches a turnstile with render, review, and delivery slots; only verified tokens open it.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Define completion in observable terms before relying on a status label.

**Gate:** Use task-appropriate criteria; do not imply all projects need the same approval chain.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, interactive_branch, prediction_quiz, mechanism_cutaway, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU060 The Retrospective Rewinds One Frame

A team wants a recurring failure to stop, but a retrospective jumps from the outcome straight to blame. Rewinding a concrete timeline exposes a missing check and yields one testable process adjustment.

**Hook:** What happened just before the mistake became expensive?

**Visible action:** A timeline rewinds to a missing checkpoint; an alternate run adds the checkpoint and displays a measurable result.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Review a sequence of events and test a specific change instead of guessing motives.

**Gate:** Avoid identifying real individuals or claiming one process change guarantees prevention.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** behind_the_scenes, side_by_side, narrative_lesson, step_by_step, interactive_branch.

### Learning home organization

#### EDU061 The Cupboard Behind the Cupboard

A cook wants to find a frequently used lid, but deep stacks hide it behind rarely used pieces. A cutaway separates visible access space from hidden storage and gives the lid a reachable, labeled slot.

**Hook:** How many things must move before this lid can leave?

**Visible action:** A cupboard becomes transparent; ghost paths count obstacles before a vertical divider gives each lid a direct exit.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Storage usefulness depends on retrieval effort as well as total capacity.

**Gate:** Use a toy cupboard; do not prescribe load limits or physical accessibility from the animation.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, step_by_step, prediction_quiz.

#### EDU062 The Keys Arrive Before the Search

A housemate wants to leave on time, but keys land in a different place after every arrival. A small landing tray at the actual entry route creates a repeatable return path and ends the fictional search loop.

**Hook:** Where did the keys go before you needed them?

**Visible action:** A stick figure enters along three past routes; dotted key trails converge on a tray placed beside the used entrance.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Put an item’s return point where the relevant routine actually happens.

**Gate:** Present one household experiment, not a universal habit or attention intervention.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, side_by_side, step_by_step, interactive_branch, silent_visual.

#### EDU063 The Drawer That Counts Footprints

A dresser wants six folded shirts to fit without hiding the bottom ones, but each fold uses a different footprint. A measured toy folding template makes the available drawer layout visible before packing.

**Hook:** Are these shirts too many, or just packed differently?

**Visible action:** Flat cloth rectangles fold along guides into equal footprints, then stand in a grid inside a transparent drawer.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Consistent dimensions can make storage capacity and visibility easier to reason about.

**Gate:** Use idealized cloth dimensions; real fabrics, drawer clearance, and preferences vary.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, step_by_step.

#### EDU064 The Cable Name Travels to Both Ends

A desk owner wants to identify a device cable, but a tangled bundle hides its destination. In a de-energized diagram, matching labels at both ends reveal the connection without guesswork.

**Hook:** Which of these ends belongs to that screen?

**Visible action:** Colored label pairs attach to a symbolic cable network; selecting one end highlights the complete path and matching end.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A useful connection label can be read at the point where identification is needed.

**Gate:** Animation is an identification diagram only; no live-electrical handling or disconnection instructions.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, silent_visual, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, side_by_side.

#### EDU065 The Maybe Box Has a Date

A household wants to decide whether a few rarely used objects still belong in its storage, but repeated indecision keeps returning them to the main shelf. A dated review box records the question for a later deliberate decision.

**Hook:** Can this decision wait somewhere that is not the whole shelf?

**Visible action:** Three uncertain objects enter a transparent box with a review date; the shelf clears while a calendar marker remains.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A deferred decision benefits from a bounded place and an explicit review point.

**Gate:** Do not imply permission to discard, donate, or sell items; ownership and sentiment remain with the user.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, interactive_branch, step_by_step, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU066 The Borrowed Stapler Leaves a Shadow

A shared household wants to know where a stapler went, but an empty drawer contains no clue. A simple borrowed-item card identifies the current holder and expected return without needing a search through rooms.

**Hook:** The stapler is gone. What could stay behind?

**Visible action:** A stapler leaves its outlined drawer slot; a borrower card slides into the outline and later returns with the object.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Shared storage can track temporary exceptions without pretending the item is lost.

**Gate:** Use fictional housemates and avoid tracking people or collecting unnecessary personal information.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step, interactive_branch, mechanism_cutaway.

#### EDU067 The Pantry Echo Purchase

A shopper wants to buy only the missing supplies, but a hidden extra packet causes another duplicate purchase. A front-facing count card and a quick stock check align the list with what is already present.

**Hook:** Is the cupboard empty, or is the last packet hiding?

**Visible action:** Duplicate packets emerge from behind boxes; inventory counters feed a shopping list that updates before the cart moves.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A purchase list is more useful when it reflects visible, current stock.

**Gate:** This is quantity organization only; do not infer food safety, freshness, or dietary suitability.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, step_by_step, prediction_quiz.

#### EDU068 The Floor Cleaned Too Soon

A tidier wants a small room reset, but clears the floor before emptying a tabletop and immediately covers it again. Sequencing the fictional tasks by where displaced objects go prevents the repeated pass.

**Hook:** Which task makes work for the task you just finished?

**Visible action:** Table objects fall into a floor work zone; a dependency arrow rearranges two task cards and removes a repeated sweep.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Sequence tasks with attention to what each step changes for the next one.

**Gate:** Show generic dry tidying only; no chemical mixing, sanitation, or hazardous cleanup guidance.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### EDU069 The Paper Lands in One Inbox

A resident wants to find a recent event flyer, but loose papers arrive on every surface. One incoming-paper tray separates unprocessed items from a small set of named destinations and makes the flyer findable.

**Hook:** Is this paper waiting for a decision, or already filed?

**Visible action:** Paper glides from door and bag into one tray, then routes to action, reference, or personally reviewed discard slots.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Separate incoming material from material that already has a decided place.

**Gate:** Use harmless invented papers; legal, financial, and identity-record retention requires separate guidance.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step, interactive_branch, narrative_lesson, side_by_side.

#### EDU070 The Cabinet Door Needs Its Own Space

A room planner wants a new storage unit to fit, but checks only the closed footprint and blocks its opening door. A swept-area overlay reveals the collision and a revised toy layout restores usable clearance.

**Hook:** It fits when shut. Can it open?

**Visible action:** A cabinet door rotates through a translucent arc that collides with a desk; the desk slides outside the arc.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A layout must account for movement and use, not just closed-object footprints.

**Gate:** Use a fictional dimensioned room; actual accessibility and installation requirements need review.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, silent_visual, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, step_by_step.

### Learning environment/ecology

#### EDU071 The Raindrop Has a Downhill Address

A raindrop wants to reach the river in a miniature landscape, but a flat map hides the ridges separating drainage routes. Raising the relief model shows its downhill path and the boundary of its watershed.

**Hook:** Which river gets this drop?

**Visible action:** A flat map lifts into terrain blocks; blue tracer drops follow slopes on opposite sides of a ridge.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Watershed boundaries follow terrain that directs surface runoff toward different outlets.

**Gate:** Review hydrology; label simplified terrain and omit flood predictions or contaminated-water handling.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, step_by_step, side_by_side.

#### EDU072 The Pollinator Has More Than One Stop

A gardener wants to understand why one flowering plant gets visits, but watches only that plant and misses the network around it. Following a reviewed example pollinator between flowers reveals interacting needs and timing.

**Hook:** Where did this visitor come from before reaching your flower?

**Visible action:** A small insect icon follows timed flower-to-flower paths while a calendar band exposes periods with few open blooms.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Pollination can be understood as interactions among organisms over space and time.

**Gate:** Select a real reviewed plant-pollinator example; do not assume all insects or flowers play identical roles.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, silent_visual.

#### EDU073 The Habitat Islands Need a Crossing

An animal icon wants to reach another suitable habitat patch, but development separates the patches in a toy landscape. Comparing possible connections shows why the value of a corridor depends on the species and surrounding conditions.

**Hook:** A green line joins the patches. Can this animal use it?

**Visible action:** Habitat blocks separate; candidate corridors appear with different width and cover markers, and a species card filters them.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Connectivity depends on the needs of the organism and the quality of the route.

**Gate:** Review a specific ecological context; do not promise a generic corridor restores biodiversity.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** interactive_branch, mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU074 The Fallen Leaf Changes Teams

A park keeper wants to trace a leaf after it falls, but stops the story when it becomes unrecognizable. A reviewed decomposer sequence follows matter through smaller pieces and biological processes without calling it disappearance.

**Hook:** When a leaf stops looking like a leaf, is its story over?

**Visible action:** A leaf breaks into labeled stages; matter-counter symbols move through a simplified decomposer network and surrounding environment.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Decomposition transforms and redistributes organic matter through ecological processes.

**Gate:** Review matter pathways and timescales; avoid implying every leaf follows one identical route.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step, silent_visual, narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz.

#### EDU075 The Food Web Loses One Knot

A museum guide wants to explain a predator’s role, but a single straight food chain hides alternative connections. Removing one node from a reviewed web highlights possible indirect effects and the limits of a simple prediction.

**Hook:** Which other lines move when this knot disappears?

**Visible action:** A network of organism nodes stretches as one node fades; direct links highlight before uncertain downstream paths appear dashed.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Food webs include indirect relationships that make ecological effects context-dependent.

**Gate:** Use a sourced ecosystem; distinguish documented effects from hypothetical outcomes.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, interactive_branch, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, myth_check.

#### EDU076 The Two Seasonal Clocks

A migrating visitor wants to arrive when a food resource is available, but two seasonal timing bands shift differently in the model. Aligning the bands reveals a possible mismatch without claiming it happens for every species.

**Hook:** What if dinner’s clock moves faster than the guest’s?

**Visible action:** Two calendar wheels rotate at different offsets; overlapping resource and arrival windows shrink or widen.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Interacting species can depend on overlapping seasonal events.

**Gate:** Choose a primary-sourced example; climate and biological responses vary by species and place.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, narrative_lesson, interactive_branch.

#### EDU077 The Shade Moves Across the Square

A planner wants to compare two public-square designs, but judges tree shade from one static noon image. A time sweep shows where shadows travel and what the simplified model leaves unmeasured.

**Hook:** Does this bench stay shaded after the camera stops?

**Visible action:** A sun indicator crosses an arc while tree and structure shadows sweep over a tiled square and a marked bench.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Shade is spatial and time-dependent; one snapshot cannot describe a whole day.

**Gate:** Review solar geometry and any heat claims; make no health or local comfort guarantees.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, step_by_step.

#### EDU078 The Hitchhiking Seed Trail

A habitat educator wants to explain how an introduced plant might reach a new site, but a map showing only established patches misses transport links. A reviewed fictionalized pathway highlights prevention questions without teaching removal or dispersal.

**Hook:** How did the next patch begin so far away?

**Visible action:** Seed symbols follow dashed routes on equipment and transport icons; arrival points connect to a source-verification card.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Species movement can involve human transport pathways as well as nearby spread.

**Gate:** Use region-specific official guidance; avoid identification, eradication, handling, or deliberate release instructions.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU079 The Shopping Bag’s Hidden Timeline

A shopper wants to compare two bags, but the picture at checkout hides production, repeated use, and disposal. A boundary slider exposes which lifecycle stages are included before any environmental comparison is made.

**Hook:** Which parts of this bag’s life did the comparison leave out?

**Visible action:** Two bags unfold into timelines; a movable analysis window includes or excludes production, use cycles, and end-of-life stages.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Environmental comparisons depend on boundaries, assumptions, use, and local end-of-life conditions.

**Gate:** Use reviewed lifecycle sources before quantitative or purchase claims; do not declare a universal winner.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, myth_check, behind_the_scenes, interactive_branch.

#### EDU080 The Night Map Gains a Dimmer

A town wants useful nighttime lighting, but a plan treating every lit area alike hides where light travels beyond its intended target. Comparing shielded and unshielded symbolic beams frames a reviewed discussion of tradeoffs.

**Hook:** Where does the light go after it misses the path?

**Visible action:** Lamp cones sweep across a path, windows, and habitat icons; beam boundaries and timing settings change in parallel panels.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Lighting design can consider direction, timing, purpose, and effects beyond the intended area.

**Gate:** Review ecology and local lighting requirements; make no public-safety or health claims without evidence.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, interactive_branch.

### Learning accessibility/inclusion

#### EDU081 The Caption That Lost the Speaker

A viewer wants to follow a scene without its audio, but the caption shows words with no indication of which offscreen figure spoke. Reviewed speaker identification and meaningful sound labels restore the missing context.

**Hook:** Who said that when neither mouth was on screen?

**Visible action:** Two offscreen speech paths feed a caption strip; speaker labels attach and a relevant door-sound cue appears.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Captions may need speaker and meaningful sound information as well as spoken words.

**Gate:** Review caption standards and actual audio; do not claim compliance from one example.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, narrative_lesson, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU082 The Chart Cannot Spend Color Twice

A reader wants to identify the two lines in a chart, but color is the only distinction. Adding direct labels and different stroke patterns makes the intended comparison available through more than one visual cue.

**Hook:** If these colors look the same, what else tells them apart?

**Visible action:** Two colored chart lines shift to equal tones; labels and dash patterns appear while their data remain unchanged.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Do not make color the sole carrier of essential information.

**Gate:** Review applicable accessibility guidance; simulated color changes do not reproduce every person’s vision.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, silent_visual, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, myth_check.

#### EDU083 The Keyboard Cursor Goes Missing

A keyboard user wants to submit a short form, but invisible focus makes the current control unclear. A visible focus indicator and a reviewed logical order reveal each next target before activation.

**Hook:** Which button would Enter activate right now?

**Visible action:** A focus ring moves through fields and controls in numbered order; an invisible-focus version runs alongside it.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Keyboard interaction needs a discernible focus position and a usable navigation sequence.

**Gate:** Review real platform behavior and accessibility standards; animation alone is not a keyboard audit.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, interactive_branch, mechanism_cutaway.

#### EDU084 The Motion Dial Keeps the Meaning

A viewer wants the result of an interface action, but a long sweeping animation is uncomfortable or distracting. A reduced-motion version preserves the state change with a quieter transition and equivalent information.

**Hook:** Can this screen explain the change without the swoop?

**Visible action:** A panel sweep is replaced by an immediate state change and a small status marker in parallel previews.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Motion can be optional while the underlying information and control remain available.

**Gate:** Review reduced-motion guidance; no flashing, simulated symptoms, or claims about all motion sensitivities.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, silent_visual, interactive_branch, behind_the_scenes, step_by_step.

#### EDU085 The Page Reads in a Different Order

A reader wants a screen-reader-friendly explanation, but visually arranged cards have a confusing underlying sequence. A linear reading trace reveals the mismatch and a revised structure aligns the intended meaning.

**Hook:** What happens when the page is read one item at a time?

**Visible action:** Numbered content blocks flatten from a grid into a queue; misplaced captions jump beside their associated figures.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Meaningful structure and reading order matter beyond visual placement.

**Gate:** Verify real semantic structure and assistive-technology behavior; this is a conceptual model.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU086 The Tiny Button Has Neighbors

A person wants to select one control, but tightly packed small targets make unintended neighbors easy to hit in a toy interface. A revised layout enlarges and separates targets without changing their functions.

**Hook:** Which target did this tap actually hit?

**Visible action:** A pointer landing area overlaps three small controls; targets expand and spacing opens in the comparison layout.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Interactive target size and spacing affect how forgiving a control layout is.

**Gate:** Review platform-specific guidance; do not invent universal pixel sizes or simulate a disability.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, step_by_step, interactive_branch.

#### EDU087 The Image Description Has a Job

An author wants to describe a chart image, but a long list of decorative colors omits its relevant finding. Defining the image’s purpose leads to a concise description and an accessible route to the underlying data.

**Hook:** What does the reader need this image to tell them?

**Visible action:** A decorative-description cloud contracts; chart purpose, finding, and data-access cards connect to the image.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Text alternatives should reflect an image’s purpose in its actual context.

**Gate:** Review the real image and relevant guidance; complex charts may need more than short alternative text.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** behind_the_scenes, side_by_side, step_by_step, interactive_branch, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU088 The Timer Erases an Unfinished Form

A user wants to finish a form at their own pace, but an unexpected timeout clears entered information. A reviewed design exposes timing, offers appropriate continuation, and preserves progress where permitted.

**Hook:** Why did the form disappear before the answer was ready?

**Visible action:** A hidden timer becomes visible; warning and continuation states appear, and entered blocks remain in a protected draft lane.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Time limits should be considered explicitly in accessible interaction design.

**Gate:** Review timing exceptions, privacy, and security constraints; no blanket requirement to retain sensitive entries.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, side_by_side, interactive_branch, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step.

#### EDU089 The Route Has More Than One Sense

A museum wants visitors to find a gallery, but directions rely only on a spoken announcement. A co-designed prototype pairs clear text, visual landmarks, and other appropriate cues so visitors can choose useful information.

**Hook:** What if the announcement is not available to you?

**Visible action:** One audio path branches into text signage, a visual map, and a reviewed tactile-cue mockup leading to the same destination.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Important wayfinding information can be offered through complementary modes.

**Gate:** Consult users and qualified access guidance; do not improvise tactile standards or emergency routes.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, side_by_side, interactive_branch, step_by_step, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU090 The Instruction Card Opens One Step

A participant wants to assemble a harmless paper model, but a dense paragraph buries the current action among later steps. A plain-language sequence with optional detail exposes one actionable step at a time.

**Hook:** Which action should happen first?

**Visible action:** A paragraph separates into numbered action cards; an optional-detail tab expands without displacing the current step.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Clear sequencing and optional detail can reduce unnecessary interpretation demands.

**Gate:** Test with intended users; plain language is context-dependent and does not guarantee universal comprehension.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, side_by_side, interactive_branch, prediction_quiz, behind_the_scenes.

### Learning consumer digital literacy

#### EDU091 The Address Bar Costume Party

A shopper wants to return to a familiar service, but a polished lookalike page imitates its branding. A reviewed example shifts attention from appearance to verifying the destination through an independently known route.

**Hook:** The logo matches. What else needs checking?

**Visible action:** Two near-identical page mockups slide apart; the destination area enlarges and a known-source route bypasses the suspicious link.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Familiar branding alone does not establish that a page is authentic.

**Gate:** Use reserved example domains only; verify current official guidance and never link to a live deceptive site.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** myth_check, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU092 The Reused Key Opens Too Many Doors

An account owner wants one compromised fictional account to stay an isolated problem, but a reused password is represented by one key fitting several doors. Distinct symbolic credentials separate the doors in the reviewed security lesson.

**Hook:** Why does one lost key fit every door?

**Visible action:** A copied key silhouette opens multiple labeled account doors; unique abstract keys replace it without displaying passwords.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Credential reuse can connect the risk across otherwise separate accounts.

**Gate:** Review current official credential guidance; never request, show, store, or transmit real secrets.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, myth_check, narrative_lesson, step_by_step.

#### EDU093 The Sign-In Prompt Nobody Started

A user wants to respond to a sudden approval prompt, but assumes every notification means an action they initiated. A timeline checks for a matching sign-in and routes the unexplained prompt to official account guidance.

**Hook:** Did you start the sign-in this prompt is asking about?

**Visible action:** A login event and approval prompt seek a matching timestamp; an unmatched prompt stops before an approval gate.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Unexpected authentication prompts deserve verification rather than automatic approval.

**Gate:** Follow current provider-specific guidance; no codes, recovery credentials, or bypass instructions.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** interactive_branch, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, narrative_lesson, mechanism_cutaway.

#### EDU094 The Flashlight Asks for a Guest List

A user wants a simple toy app function, but an unexpected permission request reaches unrelated data. A purpose-to-permission map highlights the mismatch and lets the user review the request before deciding.

**Hook:** Why would this button need those contacts?

**Visible action:** A flashlight icon connects to a permission panel; unrelated contact-card arrows light up while the stated function remains separate.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Evaluate a permission request against the feature, data involved, and trusted explanation.

**Gate:** Use a fictional app and synthetic data; do not accept permissions or claim any category is always illegitimate.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** interactive_branch, mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, myth_check.

#### EDU095 The Price Tag Has a Second Floor

A buyer wants to compare two fictional one-time offers, but one displayed price omits a mandatory fee shown later. Expanding both into itemized totals reveals the actual arithmetic before any purchase choice.

**Hook:** Is the smaller number also the smaller total?

**Visible action:** Headline prices lift like trapdoors to reveal fee blocks; full totals assemble on equal-width comparison rails.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Compare the complete stated price and commitment, not just the headline number.

**Gate:** Use invented amounts and explicit assumptions; do not infer current laws, taxes, refunds, or subscription terms.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway, myth_check.

#### EDU096 The Search Result Wears a Sponsor Badge

A reader wants to understand why a result appears, but treats an advertisement and an unpaid result as identical evidence of quality. Revealing placement labels separates the source of visibility from evaluating the underlying claim.

**Hook:** Did this position come from relevance, payment, or something else?

**Visible action:** Two result cards gain placement labels; claim evidence is inspected on a separate track from screen position.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Placement and sponsorship are context, not proof that a claim is true or false.

**Gate:** Use generic mockups; verify current labels on any named platform and avoid implying sponsorship means deception.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** myth_check, side_by_side, prediction_quiz, behind_the_scenes, interactive_branch.

#### EDU097 The Photograph Loses Its Calendar

A viewer wants to judge a dramatic claim, but a real-looking image arrives without its original date or context. The story follows source tracing until the image’s earlier setting changes the interpretation.

**Hook:** What if this picture is real but the caption is wrong?

**Visible action:** A photo card detaches from a dramatic caption; source and date cards reattach as a timeline reveals its earlier context.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Authentic media can still be presented with misleading context.

**Gate:** Use an invented event and owned illustration; do not falsely accuse an actual source or treat metadata alone as proof.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, myth_check, step_by_step, behind_the_scenes, interactive_branch.

#### EDU098 The Update Window Arrives by the Wrong Door

A user wants to keep software current, but a web advertisement imitates an update message. The narrative compares its route with the application’s verified update mechanism before any download or installation.

**Hook:** Who opened this update window?

**Visible action:** A browser ad and a generic application settings panel appear side by side; origin arrows show how each was reached.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Verify the origin and official update route before trusting an update prompt.

**Gate:** Review official vendor guidance; include no executable downloads, live security prompts, or unapproved installation.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, myth_check, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU099 The Helpful Stranger Wants a Code

An account owner wants to solve an access problem, but an unsolicited helper asks them to read out a sign-in code. A boundary between official recovery and incoming requests stops the disclosure and redirects to a verified support route.

**Hook:** What would this code let the stranger do?

**Visible action:** A masked code card approaches an outgoing message slot, stops at a verification barrier, and remains concealed.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Treat authentication codes as part of a specific sign-in, not proof for an unsolicited helper.

**Gate:** Verify provider guidance; show no real code and give no impersonation script or account-recovery bypass.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, interactive_branch, prediction_quiz, mechanism_cutaway, myth_check.

#### EDU100 The Cloud Mirror Deletes Both Copies

A creator wants a recoverable version of a project, but mistakes a synchronized folder for an independent backup. A toy deletion propagates through the mirror while a separately defined retained version remains available for comparison.

**Hook:** If both copies move together, what happens when one is deleted?

**Visible action:** Two synchronized folder icons mirror a deletion; a separate version timeline stays visible behind a retention-policy label.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Synchronization and recoverable backup or version retention are different behaviors.

**Gate:** Review the actual service’s recovery and retention behavior; do not delete files or promise recovery.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side, myth_check, prediction_quiz, step_by_step.

### Learning public safety/preparedness

#### EDU101 The Exit Plan Meets a Blocked Line

A community center wants a preparedness illustration, but a single unverified arrow crosses a blocked doorway in its fictional floor plan. A qualified review replaces guesswork with an approved, accessible route plan.

**Hook:** Who checked that this arrow leads somewhere usable?

**Visible action:** A route line stops at a blocked-door icon; the drawing gains a review checkpoint before any revised route is shown.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Preparedness diagrams need verified, locally appropriate routes and accessibility considerations.

**Gate:** Require local fire and accessibility review before publication; teach no improvised evacuation tactics. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, behind_the_scenes, side_by_side, step_by_step.

#### EDU102 The Contact Plan Has an Offline Copy

A household wants to reconnect during a disruption, but its fictional contact plan exists only on an unavailable device. A reviewed preparedness example adds an accessible offline copy and a mutually agreed contact procedure.

**Hook:** What if the plan is inside the phone that will not turn on?

**Visible action:** A phone screen darkens while a simplified, privacy-protected contact card remains visible in a designated kit slot.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A preparedness plan should account for loss of its usual communication tools.

**Gate:** Use fake contact details; obtain current official guidance and household consent before any real plan. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, side_by_side, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway.

#### EDU103 The Alert Has a Date Stamp

A resident wants to understand an urgent shared warning, but a cropped screenshot hides its location and date. Restoring those fields and checking the issuing authority prevents an old warning from being treated as a current instruction.

**Hook:** Is this warning for this place and this time?

**Visible action:** A warning card expands to expose location, timestamp, and issuer fields; a verified-source check sits beside it.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An alert’s source, location, time, and updates determine its relevance.

**Gate:** Use a fictional alert with no active emergency instructions; verify local official alert procedures. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** myth_check, step_by_step, side_by_side, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU104 The Kit Inventory Finds an Empty Slot

A household wants its emergency supplies to match an official checklist, but sealed bags make missing categories invisible. A reviewed inventory illustration opens labeled slots and records what requires attention without inventing supply quantities.

**Hook:** What is missing that a closed bag cannot show?

**Visible action:** A kit becomes a cutaway grid; checklist categories align with slots and missing entries become review flags.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Preparedness supplies benefit from an inventory matched to current official guidance and household needs.

**Gate:** No unsourced amounts, medication advice, or hazardous equipment procedures; review relevant local official guidance. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step, behind_the_scenes, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU105 The Alarm Reminder Outlasts the Beep

A resident wants a reliable reminder to check household alarms, but forgets the manufacturer’s maintenance instructions after installation. A reviewed animation links the specific device instructions to a recurring check record without demonstrating repairs.

**Hook:** Where did the instructions go after the alarm was installed?

**Visible action:** A generic alarm icon links to a manual card and dated maintenance record; unverified timing fields stay blank.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Safety-device maintenance should follow the device’s instructions and applicable official guidance.

**Gate:** Validate alarm type and local guidance; no electrical work, battery substitutions, disabling, or invented intervals. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, behind_the_scenes, narrative_lesson, side_by_side.

#### EDU106 The Wet Floor Warning Needs a Route

A facility team wants people to notice a temporary walking hazard, but a lone sign appears too late in the fictional approach. A reviewed plan considers visibility and an accessible alternative without asking viewers to step through the hazard.

**Hook:** Can the warning be seen before the choice is made?

**Visible action:** A walking path approaches a symbolic wet area; sightlines reveal the late sign and a reviewed route overlay pauses for approval.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A warning should be evaluated from the user’s actual approach and available choices.

**Gate:** Require local facilities and accessibility review; no claim that a sign alone controls a hazard. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** side_by_side, mechanism_cutaway, behind_the_scenes, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU107 The Bike Helmet Is Not a Costume

A young character wants to use a helmet in a safety poster, but the illustration treats any headwear as equivalent. A reviewed version depicts an appropriate certified helmet and refers fit decisions to current official and manufacturer guidance.

**Hook:** What makes this safety gear more than a hat?

**Visible action:** A costume hat silhouette separates from a helmet icon; certification, condition, and fit-review cards appear without adjustment instructions.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Protective equipment has intended uses and guidance that a decorative look cannot replace.

**Gate:** Require authoritative bicycle-helmet guidance; avoid unverified fitting steps, impact tests, or guarantees against injury. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** myth_check, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes, narrative_lesson.

#### EDU108 The Flood Shortcut Stays Off the Map

A traveler wants a route through a fictional town during a flood warning, but a navigation line does not reflect the warning. A reviewed safety message pauses the trip and directs decisions to current official closures and instructions.

**Hook:** Does a line on the map know what the water is doing?

**Visible action:** A route line meets a flood-warning overlay and stops; official closure information becomes a required decision input.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Navigation suggestions do not replace current hazard warnings and official instructions.

**Gate:** Use current authoritative flood-safety guidance; never show water-entry thresholds, crossing tests, or improvised escape actions. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, myth_check, side_by_side.

#### EDU109 The Emergency Address Has a Missing Piece

A preparedness class wants to practice describing a fictional location, but a landmark-only message leaves two possible buildings. A reviewed practice card adds the location details needed by the relevant local emergency system.

**Hook:** Which of these two buildings does the message mean?

**Visible action:** Two matching landmark icons appear; fictional address and entrance-detail blocks resolve the ambiguity in a simulation.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Clear location information matters when communicating an emergency.

**Gate:** Use simulation only, fake addresses, and local official instructions; never place a practice emergency call. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** step_by_step, narrative_lesson, side_by_side.

#### EDU110 The Drill Includes the Quiet Participant

An event organizer wants a preparedness exercise that includes every attendee, but a verbal-only announcement omits a participant’s stated access needs. Consultation and qualified review add appropriate communication and assistance planning before the fictional drill proceeds.

**Hook:** Who has not received the same usable information?

**Visible action:** An announcement wave reaches some figures but not all; requested communication channels connect before a review gate opens.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Inclusive preparedness requires consultation, usable communication, and validated assistance arrangements.

**Gate:** Consult affected people and qualified local authorities; never improvise lifting, transfer, rescue, or disability-specific tactics. Do not ask viewers to guess an emergency response or choose an action in a simulated emergency; any response content must come from reviewed official guidance.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes, step_by_step.

### Learning practical mechanisms

#### EDU111 The Gear Neighbors Turn Apart

A toy machine wants two visible dials to rotate together, but directly meshed external gears turn in opposite directions. Adding a reviewed idealized idler changes the direction relationship and makes the transmission path visible.

**Hook:** Why does the next wheel turn the other way?

**Visible action:** Two toothed disks mesh in close-up; a third idler slides between separated input and output gears and rotation arrows update.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Each external-gear mesh reverses rotational direction in the idealized model.

**Gate:** Verify gear placement and tooth contact; no powered-machine construction or pinch-point handling instructions.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### EDU112 The Pulley Trades Distance for Force

A model lifter wants a smaller idealized input force, but expects a movable-pulley arrangement to reduce effort without increasing rope travel. A force-and-distance ledger shows the tradeoff under explicit frictionless assumptions.

**Hook:** If the pull gets smaller, what must get longer?

**Visible action:** A load rises between rope segments while the free end travels farther; force and distance bars update side by side.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An ideal pulley arrangement trades force against distance while conserving work.

**Gate:** Declare a massless, frictionless model; no real lifting, rigging, overhead loads, or safety ratings.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, step_by_step, silent_visual.

#### EDU113 The Cam Writes a Jump

A toy automaton wants a follower to rise once per wheel turn, but a circular disk only spins without changing its height. A shaped cam profile lifts and releases the follower along a constrained vertical path.

**Hook:** Can a spinning shape draw an up-and-down motion?

**Visible action:** An eccentric cam rotates under a guided block; a trace records follower height against shaft angle.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A cam profile can convert rotation into a designed follower motion.

**Gate:** Validate idealized contact geometry; no motorized build instructions or promises about real dynamic performance.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, silent_visual, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU114 The Crank’s Straight-Line Secret

A model piston wants to slide back and forth while its driver rotates, but a rigidly attached rod jams the first design. Adding a pivoted connecting rod and a slider guide produces the intended kinematic motion.

**Hook:** How can a circle push something along a straight track?

**Visible action:** A crank pin circles, a connecting rod pivots, and a slider moves within a horizontal rail shown in cutaway.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A slider-crank linkage connects rotary motion with constrained reciprocating motion.

**Gate:** Check linkage lengths and travel; do not equate a kinematic model with a safe engine design.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, silent_visual, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, step_by_step.

#### EDU115 The Screw Unrolls Into a Ramp

A sketch artist wants to explain why a screw advances as it turns, but the helix hides its repeating rise. Unrolling an idealized thread onto a flat diagram reveals the pitch and its connection to axial travel.

**Hook:** How far does one complete turn move this thread?

**Visible action:** A helix unwraps into an inclined line; one rotation of a marked single-start screw advances by one labeled pitch.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** For an ideal single-start screw, one full turn advances by its pitch.

**Gate:** State single-start thread and ideal engagement; no load, torque, fastening, or installation guidance.
