**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### EDU116 The Ratchet Refuses a Rewind

A toy counter wants to keep its last increment, but an ordinary wheel rolls backward between pushes. A pivoting pawl engages a reviewed tooth profile so the modeled wheel advances while reverse motion is blocked.

**Hook:** What lets the wheel move one way but catches the other?

**Visible action:** A pawl lifts over each sloped tooth and drops into a gap; a reverse arrow stops at the engaged face.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A ratchet-and-pawl mechanism can permit one rotational direction while resisting the other.

**Gate:** Review geometry and assumptions; never present this toy as a brake, lock, or load-holding safety device.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, step_by_step, side_by_side.

#### EDU117 The Scissor Platform Changes Shape

A model platform wants to rise while its base stays on a rail, but pulling the lower joints apart lowers it instead. A constrained X-linkage and dimension overlay reveal the geometric relationship.

**Hook:** Will widening the feet make the platform taller?

**Visible action:** Crossed links pivot at their center while lower joints slide horizontally; platform height decreases as base width grows.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** In an ideal scissor linkage, horizontal spread and vertical height are geometrically linked.

**Gate:** Use unloaded ideal links; no real lifting-platform operation, support, or construction instructions.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, side_by_side, step_by_step.

#### EDU118 The Zipper Slider Makes Two Paths Meet

A character wants to understand a zipper without hiding the slider, but the outside view makes the teeth seem to join magically. A reviewed transparent-slider model exposes how two guided paths meet and separate.

**Hook:** What happens inside the little moving bridge?

**Visible action:** A transparent zipper slider moves over two simplified tooth rows; internal channels guide their interlocking or separation.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A zipper slider guides compatible tooth rows through a joining or separating path.

**Gate:** Review the selected zipper design; avoid repair, entrapment, or forceful-use instructions.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, silent_visual, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU119 The Siphon Drawing Has a Missing Condition

An illustrator wants a tube animation to explain a siphon, but an attractive loop alone does not establish the necessary starting and height conditions. A reviewed cutaway labels the assumptions before any modeled flow is shown.

**Hook:** Is a bent tube enough to make this water move?

**Visible action:** A transparent tube and two vessel levels gain condition labels; flow arrows remain paused until the reviewed model is specified.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A mechanism diagram must state the conditions required for the depicted behavior.

**Gate:** Use animation only; no mouth priming, liquid transfer procedure, hazardous fluids, or unsourced pressure explanation.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, myth_check, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

#### EDU120 The Lever’s Balance Point Moves

Two toy weights want to balance on a beam, but equal distances fail when one weight is twice as large. Sliding the smaller weight farther from the pivot produces equal opposing idealized moments.

**Hook:** Can the lighter block win by standing farther away?

**Visible action:** A beam pivots while weight blocks slide along labeled distance marks; weight-times-distance counters align at balance.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Lever balance depends on both force and perpendicular distance from the pivot.

**Gate:** State negligible beam mass and vertical loads; no practical lifting or structural-capacity advice.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, step_by_step, side_by_side.

### Fiction physical comedy

#### ENT001 The Runaway Finish Line

A runner wants one finish-line photograph, but a breeze keeps carrying the ribbon ahead. After increasingly ridiculous sprints, the runner stops chasing, ties the ribbon between two stationary trees, and strolls through while the exhausted photographer gets the action shot.

**Hook:** The finish line has entered the race.

**Visible action:** Ribbon bows forward; legs blur; photographer crab-walks backward; runner anchors both ribbon ends and takes one tiny step.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Check whether the target itself is moving.

**Gate:** Use a cartoon breeze and harmless running; do not imply an exercise or photography technique.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT002 One Sock, Maximum Drama

A performer must step onto a stage with matching socks, but each attempt to pull up one oversized sock pulls the other down through an absurd shared elastic. The performer finally rolls both cuffs into identical ankle bands and presents the accidental costume with confidence.

**Hook:** One sock up. One career down.

**Visible action:** Sock cuffs trade height; legs seesaw; the performer folds two cuffs, straightens, and bows as the spotlight clicks on.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A constraint can become a deliberate style choice.

**Gate:** Original fictional characters and rules; do not present the gag as an empirical claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT003 The Invisible Bench Audition

A mime wants to demonstrate a perfect imaginary bench, but each audience member imagines a different height and sits out of sync. The mime draws a visible baseline in chalk, everyone aligns their poses, and a passing janitor politely sweeps the entire imaginary bench away.

**Hook:** Please sit on the bench that is not there.

**Visible action:** Figures hover at mismatched heights; chalk line extends; everyone settles together; broom passes and the seated row tips gently onto cushions.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Shared reference points make coordination easier.

**Gate:** Keep the final fall stylized and cushioned; the invisible bench is explicitly a mime gag.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT004 The Pocket Full of Applause

A nervous speaker wants to save a little applause for later by stuffing visible clap-shaped sound marks into a pocket. The marks spring out at awkward moments until the speaker empties them into a jar, which opens during the final bow and gives one satisfyingly timed ovation.

**Hook:** I brought my own applause. It escaped.

**Visible action:** Clap symbols balloon inside a pocket, pop out during sips of water, funnel into a jar, then burst upward at the bow.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Timing can matter more than volume.

**Gate:** Sound marks behave as magical objects; do not frame the premise as real acoustics.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT005 The Umbrella Under the Rain

A figure tries to keep one tiny flower dry with an umbrella, but water collected on the canopy repeatedly tips onto the flower. The figure turns the umbrella into a distant catch basin and gives the flower a small flat roof, then gets drenched while celebrating.

**Hook:** One flower. One umbrella. Terrible management.

**Visible action:** Umbrella tilts; water sloshes; flower ducks on a flexible stem; canopy flips into a bowl away from the flower; a tiny roof unfolds.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A tool can solve one problem while creating another.

**Gate:** Cartoon water and plant behavior; no claim that flowers need rain protection or that the build is weather-safe.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT006 The Long Scarf Shortcut

A commuter wants to cross a tiny plaza, but a ridiculously long scarf keeps snagging harmless props and dragging a growing parade behind. Instead of tugging harder, the commuter walks the route backward to unwind every loop, then folds the scarf into a surprisingly small square.

**Hook:** My shortcut is getting longer.

**Visible action:** Scarf snakes around a sign, bench, and balloon stand; props trail in a tidy line; the route reverses; knots unwind in reverse order.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Undoing the last step can be the quickest recovery.

**Gate:** Use breakaway cartoon props; avoid scarf entanglement around necks, vehicles, or machinery.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT007 The Stubborn Confetti Cannon

A celebration planner wants one burst of confetti, but the hand-powered cannon only coughs out a single paper square each time. The planner arranges those squares into a giant celebratory mosaic; just as everyone applauds, the cannon releases a blank receipt labeled CONFETTI SOLD SEPARATELY.

**Hook:** This celebration is arriving one square at a time.

**Visible action:** A crank turns; single squares flutter; a floor mosaic grows; the last pull unrolls a long receipt.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Small outputs can still make something worth celebrating.

**Gate:** Use a hand-cranked fictional paper dispenser, not a pressure-device construction tutorial.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT008 The Polite Bowling Ball

A bowler needs to clear a lane, but their sentient ball refuses to bump into the pins. After failed pep talks, the ball offers each pin a tiny chair at the side, rolls peacefully to the end, and receives a handmade KINDNESS score instead of a strike.

**Hook:** This ball refuses to be rude.

**Visible action:** Ball stops before the pins, gestures apologetically, rolls out ten miniature chairs, and escorts the pins sideways.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Sometimes the goal can be redefined without a collision.

**Gate:** Bowling equipment is personified; the ending intentionally does not follow real scoring rules.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, interactive_branch.

#### ENT009 The Hat That Needed a Chair

A figure wants to wear an enormous formal hat, but its towering decorations keep bending the wearer into increasingly strange poses. The figure seats the hat on its own stool and wears only the detachable band, while guests line up to compliment the hat as if it were a person.

**Hook:** The hat has requested its own seat.

**Visible action:** Hat tower sways; wearer folds sideways; decorations rotate; hat settles on a stool; a tiny band stays on the head.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A decorative requirement does not have to control the whole person.

**Gate:** Original fictional characters and rules; do not present the gag as an empirical claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT010 The Overenthusiastic Echo

A performer wants to deliver a short greeting in a fantasy echo hall, but each echo adds an extra flourish until the hall is performing an entire musical introduction. The performer switches to a written HELLO card; the echo produces a smaller written HELLO and finally behaves.

**Hook:** Hello. HELLO! HELLOOO! We have a problem.

**Visible action:** Speech bubbles bounce along walls, grow decorative arms and musical notes, then shrink into two matching silent cards.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Change the channel when the old one keeps amplifying confusion.

**Gate:** The echo is magical and can read; do not use this as a model of real sound reflection.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

### Fiction two-person rivalry

#### ENT011 The Trophy Nobody Can Lift

Two rivals compete to decorate a shared trophy, adding heavier ornaments after every round. When neither can carry the result onto the podium, they remove one ornament at a time until only a simple cup remains, then argue playfully over who gets credit for the final subtraction.

**Hook:** Winning has become a lifting problem.

**Visible action:** Ornaments stack; trophy base sinks into a soft pedestal; rivals strain, remove matching pieces, and hold the tiny cup together.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Competition can make the original purpose disappear.

**Gate:** Use toy-scale weights and exaggerated deformation; do not demonstrate unsafe lifting.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### ENT012 The Quietest Drum Solo

Two neighbors compete to practice music more quietly, progressing from padded drums to finger taps to invisible performances. Their silent showdown accidentally attracts a crowd of readers, and they turn the rivalry into a silent concert with caption cards and choreographed bows.

**Hook:** My drum solo is quieter than yours.

**Visible action:** Drumsticks shrink into fingertips; instruments vanish; neighbors exchange fierce silent gestures; readers gather and hold up ENCORE cards.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A limitation can reveal a new audience.

**Gate:** Original fictional characters and rules; do not present the gag as an empirical claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### ENT013 The Best Empty Frame

Two gallery attendants each claim their blank frame contains the better artwork. They move the frames to capture changing real-world scenes until one sunset crosses both frames; the pair align them into a panoramic view and put the price tags away.

**Hook:** Mine is worth twice as much nothing.

**Visible action:** Frames slide over a sleeping cat, a puddle reflection, and drifting clouds; edges meet around one sunset.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Attention can make a shared scene feel valuable.

**Gate:** Original gallery setting; no imitation of a named artwork or artist.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT014 The Queue-Cutting Chess Match

Two rivals try to stand first at a booth by constantly repositioning a floor arrow that marks the queue. They rotate themselves into an elaborate spiral and discover the booth serves numbered tickets; a quiet third customer with ticket one gets served while they untangle the arrow.

**Hook:** First in line. According to this arrow.

**Visible action:** Arrow swivels; rivals sidestep and spin; a spiral queue diagram appears; ticket one flashes; both unwind the floor tape.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Optimizing a visible signal is useless when it is not the actual rule.

**Gate:** Fictional queue system; keep the rivals playful rather than endorsing queue-cutting.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT015 The Shadow Race

Two friends race to get their shadows across a finish mark first, but a movable stage lamp lets each enlarge or shrink their shadow instead of running. They eventually lock the lamp in place and race their hands as shadow animals, ending in a tied nose-touch at the line.

**Hook:** Your shadow is cheating.

**Visible action:** Lamp arcs; shadows stretch past each other; feet barely move; lamp locks; two hand-shadow animals scamper along a wall.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Agree on the rules before comparing results.

**Gate:** Stylized shadow geometry; if taught as optics, physically verify lamp, hand, and wall positions first.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### ENT016 The Last Word Balloon

Two debaters try to own the final speech bubble by stacking increasingly long replies. The bubbles become so heavy in this cartoon world that both speakers sag; they puncture all the bubbles with a tiny shared laugh, which floats away without either getting the last word.

**Hook:** Fine. But my final word is larger.

**Visible action:** Text bubbles pile into towers; shoulders sink; one laugh bubble squeezes between them and pops the stacks.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Ending an argument need not produce a winner.

**Gate:** Words have fictional physical weight; avoid mocking any speech or language difference.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT017 The Perfect Snowman Symmetry War

Two builders compete to make a toy snow figure more symmetrical, shaving away every uneven bump until the entire sculpture becomes a pebble. They stop measuring, rebuild it with deliberately mismatched arms, and discover its lopsided wave is the best part.

**Hook:** One more correction and it will be perfect.

**Visible action:** Scrapers alternate; snow figure shrinks; a ruler spans the remaining pebble; fresh snow piles up with crooked twig arms.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A pursuit of precision can erase the thing you liked.

**Gate:** Toy snow and blunt cartoon tools; no factual claim that asymmetry is universally better.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### ENT018 The Rival Weather Forecasts

Two puppet-stage forecasters compete to predict a tiny indoor cloud, secretly fanning it toward their own forecasts. Their crosswinds tangle the scenery, so they reveal both fans, label the stage weather as their creation, and collaborate on a spectacular scheduled paper snowfall.

**Hook:** My forecast is correct if I stand here.

**Visible action:** Hand fans pivot; cloud slides between SUN and RAIN boards; scenery twists; fans become visible; paper snow settles on both podiums.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Disclose when you are changing the thing you claim to predict.

**Gate:** A fabricated puppet-stage weather machine, not a meteorology demonstration.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT019 The Bigger Welcome Mat

Two shopkeepers compete with longer welcome mats until the mats cover the whole square and nobody can tell which shop is which. They cut the mats into two clear pathways and leave a shared open patch where a lost customer can finally choose.

**Hook:** Welcome. More welcome. Maximum welcome.

**Visible action:** Mats unroll nose-to-nose, overlap into a striped sea, then retract into two simple routes from one open square.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** More promotion can mean less clarity.

**Gate:** Original fictional characters and rules; do not present the gag as an empirical claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### ENT020 The Most Modest Medal

Two rivals compete to be the most humble by awarding each other increasingly elaborate medals for modesty. Buried under ribbons, they quietly take the medals off and use the ribbon pile to wrap a gift for the event volunteer, who never entered the competition.

**Hook:** I insist you accept my award for not wanting awards.

**Visible action:** Medals switch hands and multiply; ribbons coil around ankles; both unclip them; ribbons wrap one small present.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Recognition can turn outward instead of becoming another contest.

**Gate:** Original fictional characters and rules; do not present the gag as an empirical claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

### Fiction cooperation

#### ENT021 Two Ends of the Map

Two travelers each hold half a route map, but each half is unreadable alone and they refuse to let go of their section. They walk side by side to align the torn edges, discover the destination lies between their starting points, and turn together.

**Hook:** We each have half the answer.

**Visible action:** Map halves slide and rotate; torn edges meet; a dotted route crosses the seam; both travelers pivot in unison.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Combining partial information can change the plan.

**Gate:** Use an invented location and map; do not imply a real navigation route.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT022 The Table With Three Corners

Three makers want to set a long paper table, but each keeps pulling the sheet toward their own seat and tearing one edge. They add a fourth empty place for the shared food, center the sheet around it, and finally sit without tugging.

**Hook:** The table is losing this argument.

**Visible action:** Paper slides in three directions, stretches into a triangle, then settles as a fourth place setting anchors the composition.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A shared reference can align competing needs.

**Gate:** The place setting is a storytelling anchor, not a literal guarantee of physical stability.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT023 The Bridge of Pauses

Two musicians must cross a fantasy bridge that grows only during silence, but each keeps filling the other’s pause. They establish a shared breath, wait together, and cross a bridge made from the quiet intervals they finally allow.

**Hook:** Every note makes the bridge shorter.

**Visible action:** Musical notes nibble bridge tiles away; both lower instruments; empty beats produce tiles; footsteps alternate across the gap.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Leaving space can be an active contribution.

**Gate:** Explicit magical rule; no claim about sound creating or destroying structures.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT024 The Giant Tiny Letter

A group of tiny postal workers must deliver an envelope too wide for their cart, and pushing from different corners only spins it. One worker turns the envelope into a shallow tent around the cart, the others hold the folds, and they roll it safely to a toy mailbox.

**Hook:** The letter is bigger than the delivery team.

**Visible action:** Envelope pivots, folds along two creases, rises over a cart, and travels as a small paper shelter.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Changing the object’s arrangement can beat adding more effort.

**Gate:** Miniature fantasy delivery; do not claim the folding preserves real mail or postal compliance.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step.

#### ENT025 The Lantern Relay

A line of figures wants to light a festival banner, but the single fictional glow bead fades whenever carried too far by one person. They pass it hand to hand over short distances, and each handoff leaves a new light on the route until the whole line glows.

**Hook:** This light can travel, but nobody can carry it alone.

**Visible action:** Glow bead dims in one stretched hand, brightens at a handoff, and leaves dots that connect into a luminous banner.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A shared task can progress through small handoffs.

**Gate:** Use magical cold light, not flames, batteries, or electrical wiring instructions.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT026 The Orchestra of One Note Each

Five performers want to play a recognizable original tune, but each instrument can make only one note and everyone enters at once. They turn the score into a moving marker that points to one player at a time, producing a short melody and a deliberately joyful final chord.

**Hook:** Everybody knows one note. That is currently the problem.

**Visible action:** Five notes collide visually; a marker travels across five lanes; instruments activate in order; all players join at the last cue.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Coordination can make limited contributions useful.

**Gate:** Compose an original melody; do not imply that one particular sequence has universal emotional meaning.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT027 The Painting Nobody Can See Whole

Four artists each work behind a screen on one section of a mural, and their incompatible horizons initially miss at every seam. They exchange tiny edge samples, align only the boundaries, and reveal four distinct styles connected by one continuous river.

**Hook:** The river stops at every border.

**Visible action:** Screens hide panels; blue edge samples pass around; artists move river endpoints; screens lift to reveal a continuous winding shape.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Shared interfaces do not require identical styles.

**Gate:** Use original abstract art; avoid named artist imitation or claims about real collaboration outcomes.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT028 The Backpack Swap

Two hikers in a toy landscape cannot reach a lookout because one has a wide pack that catches on narrow arches and the other has a tall pack that hits low beams. They exchange packs at the route junction, each takes the fitting path, and meet at the overlook with the other’s picnic.

**Hook:** We brought the right bags to the wrong paths.

**Visible action:** Wide pack wedges at an arch; tall pack taps a beam; packs switch; split paths converge at a picnic spot.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Reassigning resources can be better than forcing a fit.

**Gate:** Use toy terrain with no real climbing or packing safety advice.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### ENT029 The Audience-Powered Curtain

A puppet crew wants to reveal a show, but the curtain is tied to several short ropes scattered through the seats. Instead of one frantic operator chasing every rope, the crew invites each seated figure to hold a section and raise it together on a visible count.

**Hook:** The show starts when all of us lift.

**Visible action:** Operator runs between rope ends; ropes droop; seated figures take one each; count cards flip; curtain rises evenly.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Distributing a task can make its timing manageable.

**Gate:** Fictional lightweight puppet curtain; not a real rigging or crowd-participation safety design.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT030 The Last Patch of Shade

Three travelers compete to fit beneath one tiny floating shade disc, repeatedly leaving someone exposed in the story’s harsh stylized sun. They stop crowding under it and use three sticks to tilt the disc toward a nearby wall, creating one long stripe of shade where all can rest.

**Hook:** There is enough shade, just in the wrong shape.

**Visible action:** Disc hovers; figures shuffle; support sticks reposition; disc tilts; a long shadow slides along the ground.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Changing the geometry can change who fits.

**Gate:** Shadow expansion is stylized; validate a physical light model before using this as an optics lesson or heat-safety advice.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

### Fiction miniature adventure

#### ENT031 Across the Breakfast Canyon

A crumb-sized explorer must return a fallen sugar crystal to a bowl, but a cereal bowl’s milk-filled moat blocks the route. The explorer sails in an empty oat shell, steers with a paper splinter, and uses a spoon already spanning the rim as a final ramp.

**Hook:** Breakfast is now a survival-sized map.

**Visible action:** Oat shell rocks on milk; paper paddle sweeps; a spoon reflects the explorer; sugar crystal rolls gently into the bowl.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A familiar surface can become a new landscape at another scale.

**Gate:** Keep the traveler fantastical; do not encourage placing toys, paper, or unknown objects in food.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT032 The Sweater Mountain Rescue

A tiny traveler wants to retrieve a lost flag caught on a sweater, but every knitted loop looks like the same hill. They follow a single contrasting thread through the fabric, reach the flag, and discover the mountain rises only because a sleeping pet is breathing beneath it.

**Hook:** This mountain is breathing.

**Visible action:** Traveler climbs yarn loops; colored thread snakes between stitches; fabric rises and falls; camera widens to a resting pet-shaped silhouette.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A reliable trail can help in a repeating landscape.

**Gate:** Toy-scale fantasy; no climbing, pet handling, or textile safety instruction.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT033 The Printer-Paper Waterfall

A paper-sized messenger must carry a seal across a desk before a harmless sheet dispenser fills it with blank pages. They surf one outgoing page, leap into its curling edge, and arrive on the destination tray inside a perfectly folded paper pocket.

**Hook:** The floor arrives one sheet at a time.

**Visible action:** Sheets emerge, curl, and stack; messenger slides along a page; one corner folds into a pocket that lands on a tray.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A moving obstacle can sometimes supply the route.

**Gate:** Use a fictional low-force paper dispenser, never a person inside a real printer or moving machine.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT034 The Button Garden Expedition

A thimble-sized gardener needs to plant one bright seed in a neglected button box, but the smooth button surfaces offer nowhere to root. They discover a thread-filled buttonhole, nest the seed in it, and grow a fictional thread-flower that ties the scattered buttons into a garden path.

**Hook:** Every island has holes. One is a home.

**Visible action:** Buttons tilt like islands; seed slides off polished surfaces; it settles in a threaded hole; yarn petals unfurl.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A feature that looks like a gap can become the useful place.

**Gate:** The seed and thread-flower are magical; not a horticultural claim or child-safe craft instruction.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT035 The Library Shelf Migration

A tiny librarian must move a paper village before a borrowed book leaves the shelf, but every house is stuck to a different page. They fold the houses flat, load them into the book’s bookmark, and unfurl the village on the neighboring volume just before the book is lifted.

**Hook:** Our entire town is due back today.

**Visible action:** Paper roofs collapse; houses slide into a wide bookmark; bookmark bridges a gap; village pops open on a second spine.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Portability can be designed into a home for a story.

**Gate:** Use fictional books and paper inhabitants; avoid damaging real library property in any practical adaptation.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes, step_by_step.

#### ENT036 The Teacup Lighthouse

A tiny sailor wants to guide a lost toy boat across a dark tabletop puddle, but reflections make three identical cup handles look like harbor entrances. The sailor rotates a patterned saucer under one cup, creating a distinctive moving reflection that the boat follows home.

**Hook:** Three harbors. Only one is real.

**Visible action:** Boat circles reflected handles; saucer rotates; striped reflection sweeps the puddle; the boat follows the unique signal.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Distinctive signals can distinguish otherwise similar landmarks.

**Gate:** Stylized tabletop reflections; do not present as maritime navigation guidance.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT037 The Pencil-Shaving Spiral

A miniature courier must climb to a desk shelf after the ladder is removed. A curled pencil shaving keeps springing open underfoot, so the courier winds it around a thick marker to make a spiral path, delivers the parcel, and leaves the new path for the next traveler.

**Hook:** The only staircase is trying to uncurl.

**Visible action:** Wood curl flexes; courier wraps it around a marker; a spiral walkway forms; parcel moves upward step by step.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A support can turn a floppy material into a usable shape.

**Gate:** Impossible miniature loads; no recommendation to make a load-bearing structure from pencil shavings.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT038 The Sock-Drawer Northern Lights

A tiny explorer seeks a colorful glow at the back of a sock drawer, but every opening floods the scene with harsh light and rearranges the terrain. They mark a path with paired socks, wait for the drawer to settle, and find an original glow-in-the-dark star patch casting the mysterious colors.

**Hook:** The sky changes every time the drawer opens.

**Visible action:** Drawer slides; sock hills tumble gently; paired-sock markers align; a stitched star patch reveals its glow in the dark.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A mystery can have a small, local source.

**Gate:** Glow colors and scale are stylized; avoid implying that ordinary fabric produces light.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT039 The Record-Groove Express

A toy traveler must reach a label at the center of a slowly turning oversized record, but walking straight keeps carrying them sideways. They ride a fictional groove-following sled inward, step onto the stationary display spindle platform, and discover the parcel was a tiny dance invitation.

**Hook:** The road goes around. The address is in the middle.

**Visible action:** Record turns slowly; footsteps arc; a sled follows an inward spiral; traveler dismounts at a fixed center platform.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A route can follow the landscape instead of fighting it.

**Gate:** Use an invented oversized display model, not a real turntable; do not claim actual record grooves form a practical ride.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT040 The Umbrella-Rib Observatory

A tiny astronomer wants to chart a pretend night sky inside an open umbrella, but each attempt to climb shifts the canopy and hides another star sticker. They lie flat in a suspended paper hammock between two ribs and finally see the full constellation, which outlines their missing hat.

**Hook:** The universe is the inside of an umbrella.

**Visible action:** Canopy tilts; star stickers slide out of view; paper hammock swings into the center; constellation lines reveal a hat shape.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A different viewpoint can expose the whole pattern.

**Gate:** Invented constellation and miniature umbrella rig; not an astronomy identification lesson or real climbing apparatus.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

### Fiction block-world chain reactions

#### ENT041 The Staircase That Unpacks Itself

A cubic traveler needs to reach a platform, but the staircase is stored as a compact accordion tower. Pulling a clearly marked top tab releases sequential hinges, and each unfolding tread frees the next until a walkable stair reaches the platform; the final step unfolds into a welcome mat.

**Hook:** The stairs are still in their box.

**Visible action:** Top tab slides; hinged block pairs rotate in sequence; treads land on supports; last panel flips into a mat.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Sequenced dependencies can make a compact object deploy in order.

**Gate:** Fictional low-force hinges; no engineering claim about real load-bearing stairs.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT042 The Rain-Sorting Rooftops

A block village wants to fill three decorative ponds, but one roof sends all toy rain beads into the first basin. As each basin reaches its marked level, a floating block tips a channel toward the next, ending with a row of tiny flags instead of an overflowing plaza.

**Hook:** Every drop keeps choosing the same address.

**Visible action:** Beads roll down roofs; floats rise; channels tilt one after another; three ponds fill to marks; flags lift.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A sequence can redirect a flow when each stage is ready.

**Gate:** Toy beads stand in for rain; model the mechanism consistently and avoid flood-control or water-safety claims.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz.

#### ENT043 The Festival of Falling Curtains

A block troupe needs to reveal five stage scenes in order, but all curtains are tangled on one shared rail. They separate the rail into staggered tracks so each lowered curtain exposes the release tab for the next, revealing a traveling story whose final scene is the waiting audience.

**Hook:** Pull one curtain. Reveal the next job.

**Visible action:** Block curtains slide down separate tracks; hidden tabs appear sequentially; five dioramas open; last mirror-like panel reflects simplified audience figures.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Reveal only the next actionable step when order matters.

**Gate:** Miniature fictional stage system; not a real overhead rigging plan.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step.

#### ENT044 The Ice-Cube Post Office

A square courier must send three sealed parcels across a model ice shelf, but a single frozen conveyor is locked. Warm-colored fantasy tiles thaw one latch at a time, letting the conveyor advance in short sections until the parcels reach insulated nests and the courier receives a thank-you snowflake.

**Hook:** The mail route has frozen mid-sentence.

**Visible action:** Glow tiles light in sequence; transparent latches soften; belt sections advance; parcels settle into nests; a snowflake card unfolds.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A stalled process can be restarted section by section.

**Gate:** Explicit fantasy thaw tiles; no claim about real ice melting rates, refrigeration, or mail handling.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT045 The Block City Night Wave

A caretaker wants to dim a toy city at bedtime, but the control is on a roof across the map. A prearranged sequence of mechanical shutters closes one window and exposes a reflector to the next, carrying a visible dimming wave across the city while leaving a moonlit path home.

**Hook:** Bedtime is crossing town one window at a time.

**Visible action:** Shutters descend; tiny reflectors rotate; the next latch releases; lighted windows fade along a serpentine route; a path remains lit.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A sequence can carry a signal while preserving one needed exception.

**Gate:** Use an internally defined optical-mechanical toy rule; not real electrical or building-safety guidance.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT046 The Returning Picnic Table

A block family needs a picnic table on a bare square, but every piece is stored beneath a different paving tile. Opening the first compartment raises a leg that unlocks the next compartment; the final tabletop rotates into place, and the now-empty compartments become matching seats.

**Hook:** The park is hiding our lunch furniture.

**Visible action:** Ground tiles flip; legs rise and latch; a tabletop rotates; remaining cavities lift seat blocks to ground level.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A deployment sequence can give emptied spaces a second purpose.

**Gate:** Clearly a toy world; no safe real furniture specification is implied.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT047 The Dandelion Launch Network

A block gardener wants to scatter square fantasy seeds across an empty grid, but one launcher keeps dropping them in a pile. Landing seeds depress miniature release platforms for preloaded launchers farther away, spreading a bounded zigzag trail until the final seed raises a miniature garden sign.

**Hook:** The first seed has to start the next journey.

**Visible action:** Seed cubes arc between soft platforms; platforms dip; preloaded cartoon arms release in sequence; a zigzag planting pattern fills the grid.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A chain can distribute outputs instead of piling them at its start.

**Gate:** Fantasy block seeds and preloaded low-force toy launchers; show finite stored energy, not perpetual motion. No ecology, germination, or weapon-building claims.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz.

#### ENT048 The Spiral Library Return

A block librarian wants to return a ring of oversized books to shelves, but the aisle is too narrow for a cart. Each shelving tray rotates into an empty position and frees the next tray, circulating one vacancy around the ring until all books land beside their matching symbols.

**Hook:** We only have one empty space.

**Visible action:** One empty tray rotates; books travel in a ring; shelf symbols line up; the vacancy returns to its original bay.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An empty position can be a useful resource in a constrained sequence.

**Gate:** Fictional rotating shelf model; validate every intermediate clearance before rendering the mechanism.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step.

#### ENT049 The Kite That Rebuilt the Skyline

A cubic festival organizer must raise a skyline-shaped banner, but the folded display lies in a heap. A tethered fantasy kite pulls a guided cable through successive counterweighted facades, raising each panel to its stop until their shadows spell the festival’s invented name.

**Hook:** The skyline is packed flat. The kite is clocking in.

**Visible action:** Kite climbs on a fixed path; cable runs through visible guides; facades pivot upright in sequence; their shadows align into letters.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** The order of assembly can be part of the spectacle.

**Gate:** Toy fantasy kite and architecture; no real rigging, aviation, or load-bearing instruction.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT050 The Marble Museum Tour

A block curator needs one glowing marble to illuminate a series of exhibits, but the last room is unreachable by the original track. Each arriving marble activates a turntable that aligns the next track segment, and the final turntable routes it back through the ceiling as the museum’s glowing exit sign.

**Hook:** One marble. Every exhibit gets a moment.

**Visible action:** Marble rolls through lit display bays; turntables rotate between fixed stops; a final ramp rises; marble rests inside EXIT letters.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A route can change its configuration as a traveler advances.

**Gate:** Fictional mechanism with explicit energy sources and ramp geometry required in production; no perpetual-motion implication.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, prediction_quiz.

### Fiction mechanical puzzles

#### ENT051 The Three-Lamp Switch Dance

A toy technician must turn three dark lamps on. Left toggles lamps 1 and 2, middle toggles 2 and 3, and right toggles 1 only; after noisy guesswork, the technician presses middle then right, lighting all three in exactly two presses and revealing a tiny stage.

**Hook:** Two presses. Three lamps. Which two?

**Visible action:** Labeled switches depress; lamp states flip; a two-row state diagram appears; middle then right produces three lit bulbs.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Track the state changes rather than the switch names.

**Gate:** Verified from initial state 000: middle gives 011 and right gives 111; no single available switch lights all three. Show these exact toggle rules.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT052 The Lift to Floor Seven

A toy lift starts at floor 0 and must reach floor 7 using a +3 button and a -2 button, without leaving floors 0 through 9. The operator tries four moves in the order +3, +3, -2, +3, yielding 0, 3, 6, 4, 7; the lift opens to a tiny rooftop garden.

**Hook:** Up three. Down two. Can we land on seven?

**Visible action:** Floor display changes 0 to 3 to 6 to 4 to 7; an on-screen path traces four presses; garden panels unfold.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Write the actual sequence before calling a route impossible.

**Gate:** Verified four-press route. Equation 3u-2d=7 has shortest nonnegative solution u=3,d=1; all listed floors stay within bounds.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT053 The Token Ferry

Three round tokens need to cross a model gap in a ferry that carries at most two. A token must ride the ferry on every trip; after overfilling fails, two cross, one returns, and the last pair crosses, leaving all three at the far platform in three trips.

**Hook:** The boat needs a passenger, even coming back.

**Visible action:** Two tokens board; ferry glides right; one token rides back; the remaining pair travels right; three tokens line up at the destination.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Count the return trip as part of the plan.

**Gate:** Verified three-trip schedule for three tokens, capacity two, and at least one rider per trip. This is a toy state puzzle, not real boating advice.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT054 The Five-Block Balance

A maker must place blocks of weights 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5 on a toy balance while leaving exactly one block unused. They set 1+5 opposite 2+4, leaving 3 as the prize pedestal; both sides total 6 and the final block displays the answer.

**Hook:** Use four blocks. Leave one out. Make it level.

**Visible action:** Numbered blocks move between pans; totals appear; 1 and 5 settle opposite 2 and 4; unused 3 rises as a pedestal.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Equal totals can come from different combinations.

**Gate:** Verified 1+5=2+4=6 with one unused block. Present this as one valid solution, not the only solution; ideal equal-arm balance model.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT055 The One-Cell Traffic Jam

Three labeled tiles A, B, C occupy slots 1, 2, 3 on a four-position ring with slot 4 empty. A caretaker must move A to slot 2 while retaining clockwise tile order; C slides to 4, B to 3, and A to 2, leaving the vacancy at slot 1 without any tile passing another.

**Hook:** Nobody can pass. One space can travel.

**Visible action:** C slides from 3 to 4; B slides from 2 to 3; A slides from 1 to 2; the empty slot finishes at 1.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Moving the empty space can move the whole arrangement.

**Gate:** Use exactly the verified adjacent sequence C:3→4, B:2→3, A:1→2. The tiles preserve their cyclic order; no swapping or hidden lift is allowed.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT056 Two Reversals Make a Return

A stage mechanic wants a pointer to turn clockwise, but two meshed toy gears reverse the input direction. They insert a third gear between input and pointer, turn the input clockwise, and watch the pointer rotate clockwise too, landing on a tiny applause sign.

**Hook:** Why is the pointer going the wrong way?

**Visible action:** Two gears mesh and turn oppositely; a center idler slides into a new spaced layout; three gear arrows alternate directions.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Count each reversal in a simple gear train.

**Gate:** Verified external spur-gear direction parity: two meshes preserve input direction. Gear centers, compatible tooth pitch, and clearances must be consistent; this is a simplified gear-train model, not a fabrication guide.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT057 The Two-Weight Elevator

A puppet needs to lower a toy platform gently, but the two removable counterweight blocks together outweigh it and one alone underbalances it in the simplified model. The puppet transfers a small labeled trim block to the platform, brings the stated totals into balance, and uses a visible hand crank for controlled movement.

**Hook:** The lift keeps choosing a side.

**Visible action:** Numbered weights hang from a visible cable; totals compare; trim block moves; crank rotates; platform descends one marked stop.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A balance condition and a motion-control mechanism are separate questions.

**Gate:** Define all masses and friction if used educationally; no numerical answer is asserted here. Not a real elevator, hoist, or suspended-load construction guide.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step.

#### ENT058 The Spiral Lock That Shows Its Work

A keeper must release a toy treasure tray using three concentric rings, but random turns keep hiding the exit slot. A cutaway reveals that every ring needs its notch at the top; the keeper aligns the outer, middle, and inner notches and slides out a single celebratory biscuit.

**Hook:** The lock is not guessing. We are.

**Visible action:** Opaque cover becomes transparent; three notches rotate independently to a top marker; a straight pin lifts; tray slides forward.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Make the hidden condition visible before trying more moves.

**Gate:** Invented toy notch puzzle, not a real security-device bypass; production must show a consistent pin path and independent ring motion.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, mechanism_cutaway, step_by_step.

#### ENT059 The Nine-Tile Mosaic

An artist must fill a 3-by-3 board with nine tiles so each row shows one circle, one square, and one triangle. The first attempt repeats a circle; they rearrange rows as circle-square-triangle, square-triangle-circle, triangle-circle-square, producing rows and columns with one of each.

**Hook:** Every row wants all three shapes.

**Visible action:** Tiles slide into nine cells; duplicate symbols flash; rows rotate into a Latin-square pattern; matching columns illuminate.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Check constraints in both directions when the puzzle promises both.

**Gate:** Verified 3×3 Latin square; each row and column contains C,S,T exactly once. If only rows are required, the displayed answer is valid but not unique.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, side_by_side.

#### ENT060 The Finite Spring Finale

A maker wants a toy theater to run forever on one wound spring, but the scene slows after each cycle. A cutaway shows the finite winding unwinding; the maker adds a visible audience hand crank and relabels the show HELP KEEP IT GOING, turning failure into participation.

**Hook:** The machine works forever. Until now.

**Visible action:** Spring unwinds; stage carousel slows; winding indicator reaches zero; an external crank appears; audience turns it and the scene resumes.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Show the input that keeps a mechanism moving.

**Gate:** The energy-accounting lesson requires primary physics-source review before publication. Avoid numerical efficiency claims or treating this stylized model as a validated device.

**Evidence class:** `requires_primary_source_review`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, myth_check, mechanism_cutaway.

### Fiction perception and hidden objects

#### ENT061 The Missing Circle Is a Hole

A museum guard must find the circle stolen from a three-shape display, but every search focuses on loose objects. Turning on a lamp reveals that the circle is the empty hole between two curved display pieces; the guard hangs the FOUND label around the gap.

**Hook:** We found every object. Where is the circle?

**Visible action:** Guard lifts a square and triangle, searches behind plinths, shifts a lamp, and frames the circular negative space with a label.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Look at the spaces between objects as well as the objects.

**Gate:** Verify that the final silhouette is genuinely circular in the rendered view; no general claim about visual perception performance.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, silent_visual.

#### ENT062 The Giant Footprint Close-Up

A scout wants to identify a supposed giant after discovering an enormous three-toed print, but every proposed monster contradicts the next clue. The camera finally pulls back to show a tiny scout examining the end of a garden fork pressed into clay, and the scout updates the sketch to FORK.

**Hook:** Whatever made this has three terrible toes.

**Visible action:** Scout measures the print; monster doodles grow; camera pulls back; a fork-shaped tool rests beside the matching clay impression.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Scale and framing can change an interpretation.

**Gate:** Use a toy fork and clay model; the reveal must match the footprint geometry exactly.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT063 The Camouflage Coat Check

A coat-check attendant must find a customer’s checkerboard coat, but it vanishes against the identical wall pattern. Instead of repainting anything, the attendant slides a plain screen behind the rack and every coat becomes visible; the customer had also blended into the opposite wall.

**Hook:** Your coat is here. So are you. Probably.

**Visible action:** Patterned coats overlap the wall; a blank screen travels behind the rack; silhouettes emerge; customer steps away from another matching pattern.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Changing the background can reveal the subject.

**Gate:** Demonstrate only the specific rendered contrast; do not claim universal camouflage or accessibility results.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, silent_visual.

#### ENT064 The Shadow With an Extra Arm

A stagehand must find a supposed extra performer shown in a wall shadow, but the cast count is correct. A side view reveals that a coat-rack branch overlaps one actor’s shadow; moving the rack separates the silhouettes and gives the branch its own tiny bow.

**Hook:** We hired three people. The wall hired four.

**Visible action:** Three actors pose; an extra shadow arm waves as a rack rolls; side view exposes overlap; rack moves away and shadows separate.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** One silhouette can combine several separate sources.

**Gate:** Check actual lamp and object positions so the reveal explains the visible shadow without a continuity cheat.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side.

#### ENT065 The Treasure Beneath the Caption

A treasure hunter searches a minimalist stage for a tiny key, but every explanatory caption lands directly over it. The hunter grabs the floating SEARCH HERE text and moves it aside, revealing the key; the final label reads MAYBE LESS TEXT and stays safely in the corner.

**Hook:** The clue is being covered by the clue.

**Visible action:** Hunter circles stage; captions bounce onto the key; text is dragged upward; key glints; final label shrinks into a corner.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Explanations can obstruct the thing they explain.

**Gate:** Make the hidden key present from the opening frame; do not falsely imply a missing object was visible earlier.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT066 The Four Rotations Portrait

A courier must identify a face on an oddly arranged tile, but it looks like scattered marks at first. They rotate the same tile by quarter turns; at the third orientation two marks read as eyes and a curve as a smile, matching the recipient, who has been standing sideways beside them.

**Hook:** The address is a face with the wrong way up.

**Visible action:** One unchanged tile rotates in 90-degree steps; marks settle into a face; camera reveals recipient leaning sideways; both straighten.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Orientation can make an intended pattern easier to recognize.

**Gate:** Keep mark positions unchanged across all rotations and use an original simple face; no claim about face-recognition science.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, silent_visual.

#### ENT067 The Ten Squares in the Window

A window cleaner must count every square in a 2-by-2 square window, but the label demanding TEN is wrong. They outline the four small squares and the single large square, replace TEN with FIVE, and reveal that the boss had accidentally counted the same window’s reflection too.

**Hook:** The sign says ten. Count the glass yourself.

**Visible action:** Four cell outlines flash; the outer frame highlights; five counters appear; a reflected duplicate slides into view; incorrect sign is corrected.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Check what is being counted, including duplicates and larger shapes.

**Gate:** A 2×2 grid contains 4 unit squares plus 1 size-2 square, total 5. Reflection creates a second image, not five additional physical window squares; title is intentionally the mistaken claim.

**Evidence class:** `mathematically_checkable`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, side_by_side.

#### ENT068 The Vanishing Stripe Train

A miniature detective wants to find which of three stationary striped boxes contains a toy train, but all look flat against striped scenery. They move a small viewing window sideways; parallax exposes the near box’s raised wheels, and the train rolls out only after being correctly identified.

**Hook:** Nothing is moving. So why did that stripe shift?

**Visible action:** Viewing aperture slides; foreground stripe edges shift relative to the background; tiny wheels emerge; one box unfolds into a toy train.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Changing viewpoint can reveal depth hidden in one view.

**Gate:** Parallax must be physically consistent in the scene; avoid claims about diagnosing vision or universal human perception.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, mechanism_cutaway, side_by_side.

#### ENT069 The Lost Star in the Dance

A choreographer must find a star badge among five moving dancers, but rapid position swaps make guessing unreliable. They pause the formation at each beat and track the badge-bearing dancer by a distinct square shoe, then reveal that the star was always pinned on that dancer’s back.

**Hook:** Follow the shoe, not the sparkle.

**Visible action:** Five figures swap marked positions; square shoe remains consistent; freeze frames show the path; final dancer turns to expose the badge.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Use a stable identifier when positions keep changing.

**Gate:** Maintain identity and badge continuity in every frame; present as this specific tracking puzzle, not a test of intelligence or attention.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, step_by_step, serial_challenge.

#### ENT070 The Arrow That Was a Fish

A traveler follows a painted arrow through a gallery, but it repeatedly appears to point back toward the entrance. Walking around the freestanding sculpture reveals that the supposed arrow is a flat fish silhouette viewed end-on; a separate clearly labeled route sign finally leads to the exhibit.

**Hook:** The directions have a tail.

**Visible action:** Camera orbits a narrow sculptural profile; arrowhead becomes a fish fin; body widens; traveler spots the real route marker.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Check whether an apparent instruction is actually a sign.

**Gate:** Design and verify the exact camera-dependent silhouette; do not rely on changing the sculpture between shots.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

### Fiction silent emotional stories

#### ENT071 The Empty Seat Sketch

A sketch artist wants to draw a two-person picnic, but the second seat stays empty and every attempt to erase it leaves a mark. They turn the mark into a flower growing beside the seat and finish a drawing that has room for both absence and today’s sunshine.

**Hook:** The seat stays.

**Visible action:** Pencil traces two chairs; eraser rubs one pale mark; a stem grows from it; artist colors a small flower and closes the sketchbook gently.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A picture can hold an absence without hiding it.

**Gate:** Treat the absent person’s reason as unspecified; do not claim that a creative act resolves grief or mental-health needs.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT072 The Umbrella Left Open

A figure wants to hurry home after rain, but notices a shivering paper bird beneath a dripping bench. They leave their umbrella open as a shelter and walk away; the next morning a neat row of paper feathers marks the route to the returned umbrella.

**Hook:** Someone still needs the shelter.

**Visible action:** Figure stops mid-stride; umbrella lowers over bird; footsteps leave; morning feathers lead back to a dry folded umbrella.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A small kindness can be shown without a speech.

**Gate:** The paper bird is a fictional creature; no wildlife rescue or animal-care instructions.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT073 The Line Across Two Windows

Two neighbors want company without interrupting each other’s quiet evenings. One draws a small hill on a window, the other continues the line on theirs, and over several nights the separate drawings become one landscape that both sit beside with their lights on.

**Hook:** One line reaches the next window.

**Visible action:** A curved line appears on one pane; another extends it; mountains, a river, and two tiny houses gradually connect across the building gap.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Connection can be gentle and leave room for quiet.

**Gate:** Use fictional neighbors and transparent removable drawing materials; no implied obligation to socialize.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT074 The Scuffed Dance Floor

A dancer wants to perform on a spotless floor and keeps wiping away every rehearsal mark. When the cloth becomes worn out, they step back and notice the remaining marks trace the dance; they leave the path visible and perform along it for one quiet spectator.

**Hook:** The floor remembers every attempt.

**Visible action:** Feet draw scuff arcs; cloth erases sections; an overhead shot reveals a winding route; dancer follows it into a final still pose.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Traces of practice can belong in the finished work.

**Gate:** Fictional expressive movement; not advice to dance on slippery, damaged, or unsafe flooring.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT075 The Tiny Birthday Candle

A figure plans a grand birthday display but only one small candle arrives. They arrange empty chairs in disappointment, then friends enter carrying single tiny lights of their own, forming a constellation around the modest cake without changing its size.

**Hook:** One little light is already here.

**Visible action:** Large empty display outline fades; one light flickers; friends place cold glowing dots around a cake; overhead view forms a constellation.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** The scale of a gathering is not the scale of its care.

**Gate:** Use flameless fantasy lights; avoid equating social attendance with personal worth.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT076 The Raincoat Patch

A traveler wants to hide a conspicuous patch on an old raincoat, repeatedly folding it out of sight. A childlike paper puppet places a tiny matching patch on its own cloak, and the traveler lets the coat hang naturally before adding a playful second patch shaped like a cloud.

**Hook:** The patch can stay.

**Visible action:** Coat folds inward; stitched patch peeks out; puppet applies a matching shape; traveler unfolds coat and adds a cloud patch.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A repair can become part of an object’s story.

**Gate:** A gentle fictional confidence story; do not frame clothing repair as a cure for shame or social anxiety.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT077 The Last Note on the Bench

A busker wants to finish a melody, but the single listener leaves before the final note. The busker plays it anyway; a folded paper bird left on the bench opens to show the listener’s drawing of the performance, and the busker places it beside the case.

**Hook:** The final note still belongs here.

**Visible action:** Listener rises and exits; musician hesitates then plays; paper bird unfolds; drawing catches a small pool of light.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A moment can matter even when its ending is quiet.

**Gate:** Use an original melody and original drawing; do not infer a stranger’s feelings beyond the visible fictional gesture.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT078 The Plant That Leaned Toward Two Desks

Two quiet office neighbors both try to place a shared decorative paper plant nearer the other as a gift, sliding it back and forth overnight. They finally push their desks into an L shape and set the plant at the shared corner, where its two paper branches point toward both seats.

**Hook:** It was meant for you.

**Visible action:** Plant slides left and right between cuts; two gift tags appear; desks rotate into an L; paper branches open toward both figures.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A gift does not always need a single owner.

**Gate:** Use a paper plant so its movement makes no unsupported claim about plant behavior.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT079 The Pocket-Sized Home

A traveler wants to unpack in a bare temporary room, but the oversized suitcase makes every object feel out of place. They find one small familiar drawing in a side pocket, tape it beside the bed, and gradually arrange the room around that single recognizable corner.

**Hook:** Start with one familiar corner.

**Visible action:** Suitcase opens over empty floor; objects hover indecisively; a small drawing is placed; lamp, cup, and blanket settle nearby.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** One familiar detail can be a starting point for a new space.

**Gate:** Keep the circumstances of temporary housing unspecified; no claim that decoration resolves displacement or hardship.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT080 The Kite String That Stayed

Two friends want to keep flying a kite together, but one must leave before the breeze ends. They tie a tiny ribbon at the last shared grip point; the remaining friend flies the kite from below the ribbon and later wraps the spool with both names before putting it away.

**Hook:** There is still a place for your hand.

**Visible action:** Two hands hold a spool; one releases; ribbon is tied; kite arcs gently; spool winds shut with ribbon visible on top.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A small marker can preserve a shared moment.

**Gate:** Use toy-scale open terrain and avoid real kite safety advice; separation is ordinary and not explained as a crisis.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

### Fiction creative-work struggles

#### ENT081 The Draft That Ate the Deadline

An animator wants to finish a short scene, but each new idea physically adds another page to a growing paper creature. They choose one page with the clearest action, park the rest in a labeled NEXT TIME box, and finish the scene while the creature curls up for a nap.

**Hook:** The draft has grown legs.

**Visible action:** Pages sprout limbs and sprawl across a desk; animator extracts one page; remaining sheets fold into a box; final shot plays on a tiny screen.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Choosing what to finish can matter more than generating more options.

**Gate:** Metaphorical creative process; do not promise productivity outcomes from a single technique.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT082 The Placeholder King

A designer wants to polish a title card but keeps treating a temporary gray rectangle as the final centerpiece. When the placeholder begins issuing absurd royal orders, the designer labels it TEMP, shrinks it to a corner, and finally draws the actual scene it was meant to support.

**Hook:** The placeholder has taken over the project.

**Visible action:** Gray rectangle gains a paper crown; layout elements bow; TEMP stamp lands; rectangle shrinks; original scene expands behind it.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Make temporary decisions visibly temporary.

**Gate:** Original fictional characters and rules; do not present the gag as an empirical claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT083 The Eraser’s Overtime

An illustrator wants a clean first line and erases every stroke until the paper becomes a transparent window. Looking through the hole reveals the messy sketch beneath, which already contains the desired pose; they trace only its strongest contour and let the rest stay loose.

**Hook:** The eraser has done more work than the pencil.

**Visible action:** Pencil touches down; eraser scrubs; paper thins; lower sketch appears; one decisive contour closes around a pose.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An imperfect attempt may contain the part you need.

**Gate:** Magical paper erosion, no literal instruction to damage materials; not a universal creative method claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT084 The Soundtrack Before the Footstep

A filmmaker wants an impressive soundtrack but keeps adding epic music to a scene where the character only takes one ordinary step. They mute the score, refine the footstep timing, and bring back one perfectly timed tiny percussion hit that makes the simple action funny.

**Hook:** The music is saving the world. He is crossing a rug.

**Visible action:** Orchestra symbols flood the frame; figure takes a tiny step; volume sliders drop; heel lands with one small sound mark.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Match emphasis to what the scene actually does.

**Gate:** Use original audio; the payoff is an editorial choice rather than a claim about universally effective comedy.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT085 The Reference Pile Avalanche

A maker wants to build one original toy creature, but reference cards pile so high that the workbench disappears. They select three abstract requirements from the pile, such as rolls, folds, and glows, put the cards aside, and build an original folding wheel-creature from those constraints.

**Hook:** I can see every reference except my own idea.

**Visible action:** Cards stack into a wall; three verbs remain on tabs; maker clears a small workspace; a new wheel-creature unfolds and lights up.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Translate references into requirements rather than copying their surfaces.

**Gate:** All shown reference cards must be original generic sketches; no reproduction or style imitation of established characters.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes, step_by_step.

#### ENT086 The Progress Bar Painter

A painter wants a progress meter for a mural, but keeps painting the meter itself until the bar reaches 100 percent beside an empty wall. They relabel it METER COMPLETE, laugh, and replace it with small checkboxes for actual mural sections, finishing one visible section before the cut.

**Hook:** One hundred percent of what?

**Visible action:** Progress bar fills; camera reveals untouched wall; label changes; three task boxes appear; one section is painted and one box checked.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Measure the work you mean to complete.

**Gate:** Fictional workflow metaphor; no general claim that checklists guarantee project completion.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT087 The Camera That Would Not Sit Still

An animator wants a dramatic reveal but keeps orbiting the camera so quickly that the small character’s important gesture is invisible. They lock the camera for one clean shot, let the gesture finish, and reserve one slow move for the actual reveal of a huge birthday sign behind the character.

**Hook:** The camera is doing all the acting.

**Visible action:** Camera-path rings spin around a still figure; motion freezes; hands complete a tiny action; one deliberate dolly reveals the sign.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Give the viewer time to read the action.

**Gate:** Editorial demonstration with original scene; avoid medical claims about motion sensitivity or attention.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT088 The Font Committee

A creator wants one readable caption, but each letter insists on using a different decorative costume. After the words become impossible to fit, the creator gives every letter the same simple outfit and lets only the key word wear a small hat, making the joke readable at a glance.

**Hook:** Every letter wants the leading role.

**Visible action:** Letters arrive in ornate costumes, bump off the line, change into simple forms, and settle; one key word gains a tiny hat.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Hierarchy can give a detail emphasis without decorating everything.

**Gate:** Use original letterforms or licensed fonts; test actual readability rather than claiming universal accessibility.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT089 The Render Queue Picnic

A creator wants to use a waiting period well but keeps refreshing an animated render meter, causing their imaginary chair to grow roots. They place a visible notification bell beside the screen, prepare a tiny picnic on the desk, and return when the completed frame appears as the picnic’s final placemat.

**Hook:** Refresh. Refresh. Still a sandwich-sized wait.

**Visible action:** Refresh icon spins; chair sprouts cartoon roots; bell is set; food-shaped paper props unfold; bell rings and completed image slides out.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A waiting process can have a clear return signal.

**Gate:** The render result is part of the fiction; do not imply any real render has been run or predict real processing time.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes.

#### ENT090 The Sequel Before Scene One

A storyteller wants to launch a ten-part saga, but every unfinished first scene splits into more sequel posters. They fold the posters into a small screening room, finish a self-contained first scene, and let the single audience member choose whether another poster comes down from storage.

**Hook:** Episode twelve is ready. Episode one is a rectangle.

**Visible action:** Posters multiply along a wall; first-scene rectangle remains blank; posters fold into seats; one short scene plays; audience points at one future poster.
