**Intended takeaway or theme:** A complete small story can support what comes next.

**Gate:** Use entirely original poster art; no claim that this approach guarantees audience growth.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, behind_the_scenes, serial_challenge.

### Fiction everyday microdramas

#### ENT091 The Mug Handle Treaty

Two people sharing a tiny table both keep rotating a mug handle into the other’s workspace. They mark a small neutral landing spot between their notebooks, place the mug there after each sip, and discover the table finally has room for a biscuit plate too.

**Hook:** The handle has crossed the border again.

**Visible action:** Mug rotates; pens are nudged; two notebooks slide; a circle is drawn on a coaster; mug returns to center; biscuit plate enters.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A small agreed place can end repeated friction.

**Gate:** Original fictional characters and rules; do not present the gag as an empirical claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT092 The Laundry Label Mystery

A figure wants to pair socks from a basket, but every sock carries a confusing abstract tag. Instead of deciphering the tags, they lay the socks heel-to-heel and compare shape and length, making pairs; the final unmatched sock becomes a puppet who proudly introduces the basket.

**Hook:** Every sock has a label. None of them help.

**Visible action:** Tags flap; socks fan across the floor; heels align; pairs fold together; lone sock rises as a speaking puppet.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Use the features that actually answer the immediate question.

**Gate:** This is a matching gag, not care-label or textile-cleaning advice.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT093 The Shopping List Origami

A shopper wants to remember three ordinary items, but their overfolded paper list has hidden each word behind another crease. They unfold it into an absurdly large paper shape, copy the three items onto one small clear card, and use the original as a bag-shaped prop for the walk home.

**Hook:** I made the list portable and the words inaccessible.

**Visible action:** Folded paper rotates through many shapes; three words appear separately; a plain card is written; old list folds into a decorative bag shape.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Compression is useful only if the information stays accessible.

**Gate:** The folded paper bag is a stylized prop, not a claim that it can safely carry groceries.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT094 The Elevator Button Courtesy Loop

Two neighbors each insist the other should choose the destination first, leaving a toy lift motionless through an elaborate polite exchange. One draws two floor numbers on a card, both point to the same number, and they press it together before bowing themselves almost out of the frame.

**Hook:** After you. After you. Still after you.

**Visible action:** Hands hover over buttons; bows deepen; card appears; two fingers land on the same number; lift display finally changes.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A tiny explicit choice can resolve excessive politeness.

**Gate:** Fictional lift interaction; no operational or emergency elevator advice.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT095 The Perfectly Timed Toast

A figure wants butter and toast ready together, but repeatedly prepares one long before the other in an exaggerated kitchen ballet. They arrange the plate, spreader, and butter first, then wait for the toaster’s normal finish signal and assemble one calm breakfast before sitting down.

**Hook:** The toast is ready. The breakfast is not.

**Visible action:** Plate slides late; butter crosses the room; tools collide harmlessly; a simple three-item setup appears; toast pops and is plated.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Prepare the nearby steps before the time-sensitive step ends.

**Gate:** Keep appliances normally used and hands away from slots; no cooking-temperature or food-safety claims.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT096 The Key That Lived in the Other Pocket

A commuter wants to leave on time, but searches the same jacket pocket after every distraction. They empty both pockets onto two labeled circles, spot the key under a folded note, and choose a single small tray by the exit for its return that evening.

**Hook:** I checked that pocket. I checked that pocket again.

**Visible action:** Hands revisit one pocket; objects tumble into two circles; note unfolds and exposes key; evening cut shows key landing in a tray.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Make a repeated search visible and give the item a repeatable home.

**Gate:** A fictional organization choice, not a guarantee about memory or attention.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT097 The Plant-Watering Calendar Duel

Two roommates each think the other forgot to water a decorative paper plant and add blue paper droplets to its pot. The absurd droplet pile reveals the duplicated effort; they put one reversible DONE card beside the pot and take turns flipping it as part of a pretend care routine.

**Hook:** We both remembered. That is the problem.

**Visible action:** Blue paper droplets accumulate; pot bulges with confetti; roommates compare calendars; one two-sided card flips and droplets stop doubling.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Make a shared task’s status visible to both people.

**Gate:** Use a clearly artificial plant and paper water; no real plant watering frequency or care advice.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT098 The Blanket Border Negotiation

Two friends watching a film keep tugging one square blanket toward themselves, exposing the other’s feet. They rotate it diagonally and sit closer to the same corner, then notice the sleeping toy pet has quietly claimed the warmest center fold.

**Hook:** The blanket treaty has collapsed again.

**Visible action:** Blanket shifts left and right; toes appear; blanket rotates to a diamond; friends settle at adjacent corners; toy pet peeks from the center.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Try a new arrangement before repeating the same tug-of-war.

**Gate:** Specific fictional blanket proportions; do not imply diagonal rotation always provides more usable coverage.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT099 The Queue Number on the Wrong Side

A customer wants to know when their turn arrives but keeps watching the decorative back of a ticket. Nearby numbers advance until they rotate the ticket, discover theirs was just called, and politely show the attendant; the attendant flips a sample ticket above the counter for the next confused customer.

**Hook:** My ticket seems very abstract.

**Visible action:** Display increments; ticket shows only a swirl; customer flips it to reveal a number; attendant mounts a two-sided sample.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** An error can reveal where the interface needs a clearer cue.

**Gate:** Fictional service policy; do not imply a real business must honor a missed queue number.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT100 The One-Trip Grocery Pride

A shopper insists on carrying every empty prop bag in one trip, turning into a wobbling tower of handles. They set everything down, make two easy trips, and discover that the dramatic ONE TRIP badge can be folded into a useful shelf label reading TWO IS FINE.

**Hook:** One trip. Seventeen handles. A questionable victory.

**Visible action:** Bag handles collect along arms; tower sways; bags are set down; two small loads cross the frame; badge folds into a label.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A self-imposed rule can be dropped when it makes the task worse.

**Gate:** Use visibly lightweight empty bags; no lifting limits, injury claims, or unsafe-load demonstration.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

### Fiction fantasy and science fiction

#### ENT101 The Moon’s Lost Appointment

A junior sky keeper must deliver a miniature moon to the evening sky, but the appointment card is written in shadows that disappear under the desk lamp. They read it by the moon’s own glow, find the correct slot, and slide the moon into place just as a paper owl clocks in.

**Hook:** The moon is late because its calendar is in shadow.

**Visible action:** Desk lamp washes out dark marks; moon glow reveals them; a sky-panel slot opens; moon rises; owl-shaped clock flips to NIGHT.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Read a message under the conditions it was designed for.

**Gate:** All celestial mechanics and shadow-writing are explicit fantasy; not astronomy instruction.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT102 The Robot Who Collected Exceptions

A tidy robot wants to sort every object into square or round boxes, but a teardrop-shaped keepsake fits neither rule. Instead of cutting it to fit, the robot adds an EXCEPTIONS shelf, then finds that the shelf holds the most interesting objects in the room and gives each a small name tag.

**Hook:** This shape is not in my instructions.

**Visible action:** Robot rotates keepsake against two box openings; cutting icon is crossed out; new shelf extends; odd shapes gather with individual labels.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A useful system can make room for things outside its first categories.

**Gate:** Fictional robot behavior; no assertion about actual AI capabilities, sentience, or classification fairness.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT103 The Planet With a Pause Button

A traveler needs to cross a tiny rotating planet to meet a friend, but pressing its pause button also freezes every helpful moving platform. They release pause, learn the visible platform rhythm, and ride the normal cycle around the globe while the friend holds up a patiently rotating welcome flag.

**Hook:** Pausing the problem paused the solution too.

**Visible action:** Planet stops mid-rotation; stepping platforms freeze apart; button releases; traveler boards at a marked beat and circles to the friend.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A control can disable useful motion along with the obstacle.

**Gate:** Imaginary planetary control and physics; keep movement rules consistent within the story.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, prediction_quiz, silent_visual, interactive_branch.

#### ENT104 The Dragon’s Paperwork Breeze

A tiny dragon wants a library card, but every nervous snort sends the forms flying. The librarian supplies one heavy reusable slate, the dragon writes by tracing soot with a claw, and its approved first loan is a picture book about taking one quiet breath.

**Hook:** Please complete the form without making it airborne.

**Visible action:** Paper forms flutter; dragon blushes; slate lands; soot lines form simple letters; a small card slides across the desk.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Adapt the interface to the participant when possible.

**Gate:** Original dragon and books; fire and soot are magical cartoon effects, not an inhalation or writing-material recommendation.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT105 The Comet Tail Repair Shop

A mechanic must repair a comet’s tangled decorative tail before a parade, but brushing it straight removes every playful curl. They arrange the curls into a deliberate spiral ribbon, attach a tiny parade bell, and send the comet off with its unusual tail proudly intact.

**Hook:** Your tail is not broken. It is badly introduced.

**Visible action:** Comet parks at a tiny bay; tail knots loosen; curls spring back; ribbon forms a spiral; comet glides away trailing the new pattern.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A repair can preserve character rather than erase it.

**Gate:** Personified decorative comet; no claim about real comet composition, trajectories, or repair.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT106 The Memory Cloud Weather Report

A cloud archivist wants to retrieve one happy picnic memory from a fantasy sky cabinet, but all the cloud drawers look identical. They sort the clouds by the objects visible inside instead of by cloud shape, find the red picnic kite, and label the drawer with that distinctive detail.

**Hook:** The forecast says scattered memories.

**Visible action:** Cloud drawers drift; blurred mini-scenes swirl; archivist spots cup, umbrella, and kite icons; one drawer opens to an original picnic tableau.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A specific cue can organize a fictional collection better than a vague category.

**Gate:** Magical memory storage; no medical or scientific claim about human memory or recovery.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT107 The Time Machine With a Receipt

An inventor wants to recover a dropped biscuit by rewinding ten seconds, but each rewind also undoes the machine’s own setup. They write the lesson on an external fantasy receipt that persists across resets, then stop rewinding and place a plate beneath the biscuit’s next wobble.

**Hook:** Every retry forgets why we tried.

**Visible action:** Clock hand spins backward; machine panels refold; receipt grows outside a glowing time boundary; inventor reads it and positions a plate.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A retry helps only if something learned survives it.

**Gate:** Explicit time-travel fantasy with a stated persistent-receipt exception; no actual time-travel claim.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT108 The Alien Who Bought a Reflection

An alien visitor wants to take home a shiny moon seen in a pond, but scooping the water keeps breaking the reflected image. A local artist paints a small original moon card; the alien trades a star-shaped pebble for it and holds the card still while the real reflection ripples goodbye.

**Hook:** I would like one moon, to go.

**Visible action:** Scoop enters water; moon image fragments; alien looks puzzled; artist paints a card; card and pebble exchange; pond reflection reforms.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A representation can travel even when its source cannot.

**Gate:** Original alien design; stylized reflections should remain visually consistent, with no claim that the moon is physically in the pond.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT109 The Space Station’s Missing Down

A toy astronaut wants to hang a welcome sign, but in the fantasy station every visitor arrives from a different wall. They replace the one-direction sign with a slowly rotating ring of repeated WELCOME text, then add matching floor symbols so each arrival can orient to a chosen route.

**Hook:** Which way is the welcome upside down?

**Visible action:** Visitors enter from three axes; flat sign rotates unsuccessfully; text ring unfolds; matching route symbols illuminate at each entrance.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A shared space may need more than one intended viewpoint.

**Gate:** Stylized station and gravity rules; not actual spacecraft signage, human-factors, or microgravity guidance.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT110 The Wish That Needed a Verb

A figure receives one wish token and asks for BETTER, causing a magical helper to improve random nearby objects while missing the real need. The figure replaces the vague word with HELP ME REACH THAT SHELF, and the helper grows one small stable-looking fantasy step so the figure can retrieve a cherished drawing.

**Hook:** Better what? The wish is improvising.

**Visible action:** Objects gain absurd sparkles and extra drawers; wish text changes into a concrete request; one step appears; drawing is reached.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A concrete goal is easier to act on than a vague improvement.

**Gate:** Fictional wish logic and magical step; not practical climbing or assistive-device advice.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, interactive_branch.

### Fiction positive public good

#### ENT111 The Sign at Knee Height

A park volunteer wants all visitors to find a small puppet show, but the only direction sign is hidden behind a crowd’s shoulders. They add a second matching sign on a clear low stand and a visible path symbol, allowing both tall and short cartoon visitors to follow the same route.

**Hook:** The directions disappear when everyone arrives.

**Visible action:** Crowd gathers; overhead sightline blocks; low sign slides into open view; matching ground symbols lead to the puppet stage.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Check an instruction from more than one viewpoint.

**Gate:** Do not claim accessibility compliance from a cartoon; real public signage requires relevant standards and user testing.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### ENT112 The Shared Tool’s Return Ticket

A community craft table wants to keep one hole punch available, but it vanishes under half-finished paper projects. A volunteer adds a bright outline where it belongs and a removable BORROWED card carried with it; the next borrower finds the card, waits visibly, and receives the returned tool without a search.

**Hook:** The shared tool has entered the paper jungle.

**Visible action:** Paper piles spread; tool outline is drawn; BORROWED card travels with the punch; tool returns to its marked spot and card flips.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Make shared-resource status easy to see.

**Gate:** Use a blunt toy craft tool; no assertion that this prevents theft or meets workplace safety requirements.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT113 The Bench With Room for the Bag

A traveler wants to rest at a crowded toy bus stop, but bags occupy the remaining seats. A waiting passenger notices, moves their own bag to a designated shelf, and others copy the gesture, revealing one free seat without anyone delivering a lecture.

**Hook:** One small move makes a seat.

**Visible action:** Bags sit like silent passengers; one bag rises to a shelf; others follow; traveler sits; everyone resumes waiting.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A small voluntary adjustment can open shared space.

**Gate:** Use an invented stop and designated bag shelf; do not infer strangers’ needs or instruct obstruction of real walkways.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT114 The Library Book That Came With a Map

A volunteer wants to return scattered toy library books to the correct shelves, but abstract section labels confuse every helper. They draw a simple shelf map using the same symbols as the spines, place it on the cart, and finish the return route with a new helper confidently leading.

**Hook:** The books know their symbols. The shelves need to match.

**Visible action:** Cart stops between shelves; spine symbols compare; matching shelf map unfolds; books move into corresponding bays; helper takes the front handle.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Consistent labels can make a shared task easier to join.

**Gate:** Fictional classification system; do not imply a real library should be reorganized without staff authorization.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT115 The Repair Fair’s Little Waiting Chair

A volunteer wants to keep a toy repair fair organized, but people holding broken paper objects crowd the workbench. They create a clear intake tray, a numbered waiting chair, and a visible READY shelf; the final reveal shows one repaired paper bird flying a thank-you tag between all three stations.

**Hook:** Everything is waiting in the work area.

**Visible action:** Objects pile on bench; three station labels appear; one numbered token moves intake to chair to ready shelf; repaired paper bird flutters overhead.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Separate arrival, work, and collection so status stays visible.

**Gate:** Repair only paper props; real electrical, medical, structural, or safety-critical repairs require appropriate expertise.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT116 The Quiet Corner Flag

A festival organizer wants a place for visitors who prefer a pause, but the quiet corner is decorated with a noisily flapping banner. They replace it with a still, clearly labeled flag icon and move the paper drum parade elsewhere, leaving a calm bench that visitors can choose freely.

**Hook:** The quiet corner has a very loud announcement.

**Visible action:** Banner snaps with oversized sound marks; drum parade loops past; still icon replaces banner; parade route bends away; figures settle on a bench.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** The way a space is announced should support its purpose.

**Gate:** Fictional festival choice; no medical, sensory-accessibility, or acoustic-performance guarantee.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

#### ENT117 The Dropped Pencil Relay

A pupil-like paper figure drops a pencil beyond a row of occupied desks and cannot reach it without disrupting everyone. The nearest figure picks it up and quietly passes it down the row; each recipient points toward the owner until the pencil arrives with a tiny folded paper bow added by the last helper.

**Hook:** It is yours. Pass it closer.

**Visible action:** Pencil rolls; one hand retrieves it; successive hands pass and point; pencil lands on owner’s desk; a small thank-you wave travels back.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Small help can move through a group without becoming a spectacle.

**Gate:** Use fictional stylized figures, no real minor’s likeness or personal information; keep the object blunt and safely handled.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual.

#### ENT118 The Lost-and-Found Silhouette

A community host wants to reunite a visitor with a missing mitten, but the lost-and-found box is a jumble. They hang simple item silhouettes above open compartments; the visitor finds the mitten in the matching shape area and leaves a little drawing showing where the box is for the next person.

**Hook:** The mitten is lost inside lost-and-found.

**Visible action:** Objects spill from box; silhouettes appear; compartments sort by item type; mitten pair reunites; location drawing goes on a nearby board.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** Organization helps only if people can find the organized place.

**Gate:** No real names, contact details, or identity documents in the display; public lost-property processes may require verification.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, step_by_step.

#### ENT119 The Sidewalk Chalk Detour

A volunteer wants visitors to avoid a pretend wet-paint mural on the floor, but a single STOP sign makes everyone bunch up. They add a clearly marked alternate path around the work area and an arrow back to the original route; traffic flows gently while the painter adds the final flower.

**Hook:** Stopping is clear. Going somewhere else is missing.

**Visible action:** Figures halt at sign; queue grows; chalk arrows trace a loop; figures follow it; returning arrow joins the old route; painter finishes.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** When blocking a route, show a usable next option.

**Gate:** Toy-scale staging only; real street closures, crowd routing, and accessibility require authorized planning and applicable standards.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, mechanism_cutaway.

#### ENT120 The Community Noticeboard’s Empty Square

A neighborhood noticeboard fills with competing oversized announcements, hiding a small request for spare drawing paper. A volunteer gives each notice an equal marked square and reserves one clearly labeled REQUESTS area; the final square receives a bundle of donated blank paper and a tiny original thank-you sketch.

**Hook:** The smallest request is under the largest headline.

**Visible action:** Posters overlap; grid lines appear; notices shrink into equal cells; paper request becomes visible; blank sheets arrive in a nearby collection tray.

**Intended takeaway or theme:** A shared information space can make modest requests visible.

**Gate:** Use fictional notices and no personal contact data; equal visual space is a story choice, not a complete moderation or public-policy solution.

**Evidence class:** `fictional_demonstration`. **Original format tags:** narrative_lesson, silent_visual, side_by_side.

## Appendix E All 1024 adversarial review questions

Use this bank as a menu, not a requirement to review every question on every film. Each entry keeps its stable ID, question, failure hypothesis, practical check/fix, severity and modality/stage filters. The evidence contract in the main guide applies to every entry. Tags are candidate filters; they cannot make an irrelevant question applicable or turn missing evidence into a pass. Detailed machine-readable records remain in the preserved source bank.

### D001 concept and design

- **AVQ-001-001** Can the first image communicate the film's central promise? **Failure hypothesis:** Opening conceals the viewing purpose. **Check or fix:** Show opening without its title to a fresh viewer. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-002** Does the chosen visual style make the subject easier to understand? **Failure hypothesis:** Style adds decoration without explanatory value. **Check or fix:** Compare one beat in the proposed and simpler styles. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-003** Is the visual metaphor consistent with the actual mechanism? **Failure hypothesis:** Metaphor teaches a false relationship. **Check or fix:** Map each metaphor element to its intended meaning. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-004** Would removing the hero character improve the explanation? **Failure hypothesis:** Host competes with the information. **Check or fix:** Test a host-free version of one dense beat. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-005** Does every recurring graphic have a stable meaning? **Failure hypothesis:** Decorative reuse changes semantic meaning. **Check or fix:** Audit recurring symbols against a single legend. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-006** Is the design distinctive without depending on a borrowed character? **Failure hypothesis:** Identity relies on recognizable protected features. **Check or fix:** Replace signature borrowed features with original design logic. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-007** Does the visual grammar permit the required emotional range? **Failure hypothesis:** Chosen abstraction cannot express the turning point. **Check or fix:** Thumbnail both the quietest and strongest reaction. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-008** Is complexity concentrated where the viewer needs information? **Failure hypothesis:** Detail budget is spent on irrelevant scenery. **Check or fix:** Rank scene elements by narrative necessity. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-009** Can the premise survive without explanatory labels everywhere? **Failure hypothesis:** Labels compensate for unreadable action. **Check or fix:** Review a label-free storyboard before refining captions. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-010** Does the world establish which impossible actions are allowed? **Failure hypothesis:** Fictional rules change only to resolve the plot. **Check or fix:** List impossible events and seed their rules early. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-011** Are style shifts motivated by story or information changes? **Failure hypothesis:** Unmotivated shifts break the film's identity. **Check or fix:** Attach a purpose to each style boundary. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-012** Does the palette distinguish functions before decorating them? **Failure hypothesis:** Aesthetic palette erases important categories. **Check or fix:** Assign functional roles before choosing accent colors. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-013** Can the design scale to the most crowded required scene? **Failure hypothesis:** Hero design collapses when multiplied. **Check or fix:** Test the largest crowd at delivery resolution. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-014** Is the hero recognizable through its most extreme pose? **Failure hypothesis:** Design identity depends on one front-facing portrait. **Check or fix:** Compare key poses as small unlabeled thumbnails. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-015** Does the setting provide useful action opportunities? **Failure hypothesis:** Scenery cannot support the scripted interactions. **Check or fix:** Mark every prop and surface the action uses. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-016** Are ornamental motifs misleading viewers about causal importance? **Failure hypothesis:** Decorations resemble clues or controls. **Check or fix:** Remove ornaments near the causal focus and compare. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-017** Is the intended genre legible before the first reversal? **Failure hypothesis:** Comic styling makes serious claims seem unserious. **Check or fix:** Check opening tone against the story's consequence. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-018** Does the visual system accommodate uncertainty honestly? **Failure hypothesis:** Polished certainty masks incomplete evidence. **Check or fix:** Design a clear visual treatment for qualified claims. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-019** Are scale exaggerations explicitly separated from measurements? **Failure hypothesis:** Expressive proportions are read as quantitative truth. **Check or fix:** Label illustrative scale where comparisons depend on size. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-020** Does the design avoid stereotypes as character shorthand? **Failure hypothesis:** Identity traits replace individual characterization. **Check or fix:** Give roles distinctive goals and behavior instead. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-021** Would an unfamiliar viewer understand the icon vocabulary? **Failure hypothesis:** Icons require unstated cultural knowledge. **Check or fix:** Ask viewers to name icons without a legend. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual, human-audience. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-022** Is visual richness earned by meaningful variation? **Failure hypothesis:** Many objects repeat the same information. **Check or fix:** Remove repeated detail that adds neither rhythm nor meaning. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-023** Does the film's aesthetic match its delivery context? **Failure hypothesis:** Subtle design disappears in expected viewing conditions. **Check or fix:** Preview under likely screen size and ambient light. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-024** Can the design support a clear ending state? **Failure hypothesis:** Ending looks indistinguishable from the starting state. **Check or fix:** Compare first and last meaningful frames side by side. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-025** Is the central contrast visible without narration? **Failure hypothesis:** Key comparison exists only in spoken explanation. **Check or fix:** Make the contrasted states visually distinguishable. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-026** Does the mood board contain conflicting material rules? **Failure hypothesis:** Mixed references produce an incoherent world. **Check or fix:** Choose explicit rules for edges, light and texture. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-027** Are designed imperfections intentional and repeatable? **Failure hypothesis:** Random defects are mistaken for handmade style. **Check or fix:** Define which imperfections belong and remove others. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-028** Does abstraction remove the detail needed to judge success? **Failure hypothesis:** Simplification hides the actual result. **Check or fix:** Restore the smallest evidence-bearing feature. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-029** Can the design accommodate captions without shrinking the action? **Failure hypothesis:** Accessibility was excluded from the layout concept. **Check or fix:** Reserve readable caption space in initial thumbnails. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-030** Does the film need all its branded framing? **Failure hypothesis:** Persistent branding consumes attention and action area. **Check or fix:** Compare a restrained framing treatment at phone size. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-031** Is there a coherent hierarchy between world and interface graphics? **Failure hypothesis:** Labels and physical objects appear equally tangible. **Check or fix:** Give annotations a consistent separate graphic grammar. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-001-032** What single design change would most strengthen the viewing promise? **Failure hypothesis:** Many small polish changes avoid the main weakness. **Check or fix:** Choose one premise-level improvement before surface polish. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** concept, script, storyboard, animatic. **Style filters:** any.

### D002 composition and hierarchy

- **AVQ-002-001** Where does the eye land before any movement occurs? **Failure hypothesis:** Bright decoration steals the first fixation. **Check or fix:** Blur the frame and inspect its dominant mass. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-002** Is the subject separated from the frame's visual center when needed? **Failure hypothesis:** Central symmetry suppresses directional tension. **Check or fix:** Compare asymmetric staging for the active beat. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-003** Does the focal point stay clear in grayscale? **Failure hypothesis:** Color alone creates the hierarchy. **Check or fix:** Inspect a grayscale frame at thumbnail size. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-004** Are empty areas supporting anticipation or merely unused? **Failure hypothesis:** Dead space weakens the action's scale. **Check or fix:** Reframe while preserving necessary look and travel room. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-005** Does the composition reserve room for the next action? **Failure hypothesis:** Movement immediately crashes into the frame edge. **Check or fix:** Storyboard the full action envelope before cropping. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-006** Do dominant diagonals point toward the intended event? **Failure hypothesis:** Environmental lines direct attention away from the payoff. **Check or fix:** Trace major lines and adjust their endpoints. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-007** Is the hero smaller than a visually irrelevant prop? **Failure hypothesis:** Scale hierarchy accidentally promotes scenery. **Check or fix:** Reduce the prop or increase hero screen occupancy. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-008** Does the frame contain two competing high-contrast centers? **Failure hypothesis:** Viewer attention splits during a single causal beat. **Check or fix:** Subdue one center until its turn to matter. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-009** Can the critical gesture be seen without hunting? **Failure hypothesis:** The gesture occupies a low-salience corner. **Check or fix:** Reposition or simplify neighboring shapes. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-010** Does the horizon cut through a meaningful face or hand? **Failure hypothesis:** A strong line fragments the focal silhouette. **Check or fix:** Shift camera height or background geometry. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-011** Are masses balanced over time rather than only in stills? **Failure hypothesis:** Moving crowds overload one side of the frame. **Check or fix:** Sample composition at action extrema. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-012** Does a close-up exclude context needed to interpret contact? **Failure hypothesis:** Tight framing makes the interaction ambiguous. **Check or fix:** Include the receiving surface or establishing insert. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-013** Are graphic borders stealing more contrast than the action? **Failure hypothesis:** Frame decoration dominates the content. **Check or fix:** Reduce border weight and compare attention. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-014** Is the negative space around the reveal preserved? **Failure hypothesis:** Surrounding detail swallows the new information. **Check or fix:** Clear a quiet area before the reveal arrives. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-015** Do repeated elements form an unintended arrow or word? **Failure hypothesis:** Pattern alignment creates misleading visual cues. **Check or fix:** Inspect repetitions at small size and oblique angles. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-016** Are important objects clipped by aesthetically convenient crops? **Failure hypothesis:** Composition removes evidence-bearing parts. **Check or fix:** Check all essential object bounds against the frame. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-017** Is the composition understandable when paused mid-action? **Failure hypothesis:** Only carefully selected endpoints are readable. **Check or fix:** Review random frames between key poses. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-018** Does the camera reveal enough of the destination? **Failure hypothesis:** Movement has no legible spatial goal. **Check or fix:** Show or establish the target before arrival. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-019** Are paired comparisons granted equivalent visual emphasis? **Failure hypothesis:** One side appears important because of framing alone. **Check or fix:** Match scale, contrast and dwell time across panels. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-020** Does perspective compression make separate objects look attached? **Failure hypothesis:** Projected tangencies imply nonexistent connections. **Check or fix:** Adjust view or spacing to separate outlines. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-021** Is the foreground framing device obscuring useful information? **Failure hypothesis:** Cinematic framing hides the causal action. **Check or fix:** Mask its overlap against required evidence regions. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-022** Does a large caption reverse the intended visual hierarchy? **Failure hypothesis:** The audience reads while the crucial event passes. **Check or fix:** Separate reading and action beats where possible. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-023** Are small supporting characters legible as intentional participants? **Failure hypothesis:** Minor roles look like noise. **Check or fix:** Group them into readable masses or give clear actions. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-024** Does a wide establishing shot remain useful after geography is known? **Failure hypothesis:** Repeated wide shots reduce emotional access. **Check or fix:** Use a closer view for the next consequential beat. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-025** Can the final composition hold without accidental edge tension? **Failure hypothesis:** Ending feels cropped or unstable. **Check or fix:** Inspect final hold with all overlays and margins. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-026** Are parallel lines almost touching and creating visual vibration? **Failure hypothesis:** Narrow gaps shimmer or appear fused. **Check or fix:** Increase separation or commit to overlap. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-027** Does the layout create a natural reading order? **Failure hypothesis:** Viewer sees the answer before the setup. **Check or fix:** Number or stage information in intended sequence. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-028** Are visual weights stable across differently colored costumes? **Failure hypothesis:** One costume dominates unrelated scenes. **Check or fix:** Compare luminance and occupied area across characters. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-029** Does the composition survive the intended alternate crop? **Failure hypothesis:** Reformatting removes the cause or reaction. **Check or fix:** Preview actual crop windows before delivery. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-030** Is the hero's gaze given enough look room? **Failure hypothesis:** Gaze terminates against the frame edge. **Check or fix:** Adjust framing to reveal the attended space. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-031** Does the composition distinguish environment scale from character scale? **Failure hypothesis:** No stable reference makes distances ambiguous. **Check or fix:** Include one consistent scale reference. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-002-032** Would removing one object immediately clarify the frame? **Failure hypothesis:** Clutter persists because every asset seems precious. **Check or fix:** Perform a subtractive composition pass. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

### D003 foreground background separation

- **AVQ-003-001** Does the hero's outline merge with the scenery at any key pose? **Failure hypothesis:** Matching values erase the silhouette. **Check or fix:** Inspect key poses over grayscale backgrounds. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-002** Are foreground and background equally sharp without a reason? **Failure hypothesis:** Every layer demands equal attention. **Check or fix:** Use controlled detail or focus hierarchy. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-003** Does background motion compete with the hero's reaction? **Failure hypothesis:** Ambient activity masks the meaningful gesture. **Check or fix:** Pause or reduce background motion during the reaction. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-004** Is a dark costume lost against a dark set region? **Failure hypothesis:** Local contrast collapses along the path. **Check or fix:** Track costume-background contrast throughout movement. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-005** Do background objects resemble extra limbs on the hero? **Failure hypothesis:** Aligned props create anatomical ambiguity. **Check or fix:** Move the prop or change camera angle. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-006** Does the hero cross a bright patch just before the payoff? **Failure hypothesis:** Background flash hijacks the eye. **Check or fix:** Reduce patch contrast or retime the crossing. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-007** Are labels visually mistaken for physical scene objects? **Failure hypothesis:** Overlay styling implies false spatial membership. **Check or fix:** Use a consistent annotation treatment. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-008** Can target objects be distinguished from background texture? **Failure hypothesis:** Texture contains target-like forms. **Check or fix:** Inspect texture without target layers present. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-009** Does atmospheric depth weaken an essential distant clue? **Failure hypothesis:** Depth treatment removes required information. **Check or fix:** Keep evidence contrast above the chosen viewing threshold. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-010** Are background crowds individual when the story needs a mass? **Failure hypothesis:** Excess individuality produces visual noise. **Check or fix:** Group distant figures while preserving meaningful identities. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-011** Do foreground silhouettes block facial reactions? **Failure hypothesis:** Framing elements cover expression at the worst moment. **Check or fix:** Plot overlap during the full reaction interval. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-012** Does scenery contain a stronger face-like pattern than the hero? **Failure hypothesis:** Pareidolia steals attention from intended characters. **Check or fix:** Simplify face-like background clusters. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-013** Are foreground edge treatments consistent with the background medium? **Failure hypothesis:** Cutout edges look pasted onto incompatible art. **Check or fix:** Match appropriate softness, grain and contour logic. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-014** Does foreground saturation overwhelm subtle background evidence? **Failure hypothesis:** Accent color makes the clue easy to miss. **Check or fix:** Rebalance saturation around the evidence beat. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-015** Is the background too blank to establish contact and scale? **Failure hypothesis:** Minimalism removes grounding cues. **Check or fix:** Add restrained ground plane and reference details. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-016** Does a background line continue through the hero as a tangent? **Failure hypothesis:** Apparent line continuity flattens the layer separation. **Check or fix:** Break or relocate the line behind the subject. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-017** Is the hero distinguishable during backlighting? **Failure hypothesis:** Silhouette merges into an uncontrolled dark mass. **Check or fix:** Preserve contour or adjust background luminance. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-018** Are distant outlines heavier than foreground outlines? **Failure hypothesis:** Line weight reverses the depth hierarchy. **Check or fix:** Normalize contour weight by intended depth logic. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-019** Does background contrast rise during narration-heavy passages? **Failure hypothesis:** Scenery competes with reading and listening. **Check or fix:** Reduce nonessential contrast during information delivery. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-020** Can a newcomer identify which objects are interactive? **Failure hypothesis:** Visual affordances do not distinguish usable props. **Check or fix:** Give important props clear placement or introduction. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-021** Does the background change imply a location change unintentionally? **Failure hypothesis:** Palette or texture shifts break scene identity. **Check or fix:** Match persistent landmarks across adjacent shots. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-022** Are foreground particles obscuring the decisive contact frame? **Failure hypothesis:** Effects conceal whether the action succeeded. **Check or fix:** Delay or thin particles at the contact moment. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-023** Does a moving background make a stationary subject seem to drift? **Failure hypothesis:** Relative motion creates unintended self-motion. **Check or fix:** Add stable grounding cues or change motion balance. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-024** Do shadows merge foreground and background into one blob? **Failure hypothesis:** Shadow design removes object separation. **Check or fix:** Adjust shadow softness, offset or intensity. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-025** Does a light outline become a distracting halo? **Failure hypothesis:** Separation aid looks like a compositing defect. **Check or fix:** Test subtler contour and local-background adjustments. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-026** Are foreground and background using incompatible perspective cues? **Failure hypothesis:** The subject appears pasted into another world. **Check or fix:** Compare horizon, vanishing directions and scale cues. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-027** Can background story details be discovered without stealing the main beat? **Failure hypothesis:** Secondary storytelling competes with primary causality. **Check or fix:** Place discoveries in quieter intervals. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-028** Does color grading erase previously good layer separation? **Failure hypothesis:** Final treatment compresses local contrast. **Check or fix:** Repeat separation checks on the encoded graded output. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-029** Are reflected subjects confused with additional real subjects? **Failure hypothesis:** Reflections lack a clear surface context. **Check or fix:** Make the reflective boundary and relationship legible. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-030** Does foreground blur hide information the viewer must use? **Failure hypothesis:** Depth-of-field prioritizes atmosphere over evidence. **Check or fix:** Keep task-relevant objects in an inspectable focus range. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-031** Can the scene remain readable on a dim phone display? **Failure hypothesis:** Low-end viewing conditions collapse contrast. **Check or fix:** Preview at modest brightness and intended size. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-003-032** Are guide lines strong enough to remain visible in the overview? **Failure hypothesis:** Navigation cues disappear when the camera pulls back. **Check or fix:** Compare the wide encoded frame at phone size and adjust guide contrast if needed. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

### D004 depth and occlusion

- **AVQ-004-001** Does every overlap agree with the intended front-to-back order? **Failure hypothesis:** Layer order contradicts scene geometry. **Check or fix:** Inspect crossings against a depth-order map. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-002** Does an occluded object return with the same identity? **Failure hypothesis:** Reappearance silently substitutes the object. **Check or fix:** Track stable object IDs through occlusion. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-003** Are disoccluded areas actually painted or modeled? **Failure hypothesis:** Camera movement exposes empty holes. **Check or fix:** Inspect maximum parallax and edge reveals. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-004** Does a hand disappear behind the prop it should hold in front? **Failure hypothesis:** Incorrect depth ordering breaks the grasp. **Check or fix:** Review hand-prop masks at contact extrema. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-005** Are transparent surfaces ordered correctly with opaque objects? **Failure hypothesis:** Transparency sorting creates impossible overlaps. **Check or fix:** Test each intersecting layer from the final camera. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-006** Does the hero pass through a foreground object without response? **Failure hypothesis:** Occlusion hides a geometric collision. **Check or fix:** Check trajectories against collision silhouettes. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-007** Do objects change apparent depth without crossing a boundary? **Failure hypothesis:** Sort order pops during movement. **Check or fix:** Inspect depth swaps one frame at a time. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-008** Can the viewer infer which route lies above another? **Failure hypothesis:** Crossing paths have ambiguous elevation. **Check or fix:** Add bridge, shadow or cutaway cues. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-009** Is a distant object larger without an established scale reason? **Failure hypothesis:** Projection cues imply impossible depth. **Check or fix:** Compare world scale and camera projection. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-010** Does parallax direction agree with camera travel? **Failure hypothesis:** Layers move as if depth were inverted. **Check or fix:** Plot layer displacement against declared depth. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-011** Are contact shadows behind the correct object? **Failure hypothesis:** Shadow stacking contradicts grounding. **Check or fix:** Review shadow masks independently from color layers. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-012** Does a mask hide more than the physical occluder should? **Failure hypothesis:** Loose masks remove visible limbs or props. **Check or fix:** Compare matte contour to the actual boundary. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-013** Are soft alpha edges revealing the hidden layer unnaturally? **Failure hypothesis:** Premultiplication or feathering creates ghost edges. **Check or fix:** Inspect compositing over contrasting backgrounds. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-014** Does depth-of-field change imply a different focal plane than intended? **Failure hypothesis:** Focus favors the wrong spatial relationship. **Check or fix:** Mark focus targets against shot purpose. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-015** Can partly occluded characters still be counted fairly? **Failure hypothesis:** Occlusion leaves ambiguous identity fragments. **Check or fix:** Review visible identifying features for each counted entity. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** puzzle, educational.

- **AVQ-004-016** Does the foreground reveal happen before the viewer needs it? **Failure hypothesis:** Late disocclusion withholds causal information. **Check or fix:** Move the reveal earlier or add an establishing cue. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-017** Are far objects clipped by a near-plane artifact? **Failure hypothesis:** Camera settings remove legitimate scene geometry. **Check or fix:** Check clipping planes at closest camera positions. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-018** Does a cutaway show interior parts in their real relative positions? **Failure hypothesis:** Illustrative separation masquerades as spatial truth. **Check or fix:** Label exploded offsets or restore spatial registration. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-019** Are stacked paper planes thick enough to match their shadows? **Failure hypothesis:** Shadow depth exceeds the depicted material thickness. **Check or fix:** Align layer spacing and shadow treatment. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-020** Does a rotating flat asset expose its lack of volume? **Failure hypothesis:** Billboard rotation reveals an unintended paper edge. **Check or fix:** Limit rotation or provide appropriate alternate geometry. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-021** Are reflections occluded consistently with the reflecting surface? **Failure hypothesis:** Reflection content ignores scene boundaries. **Check or fix:** Compare reflection mask with surface visibility. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-022** Does a moving camera reveal duplicate copies of one object? **Failure hypothesis:** Patch layers overlap as two distinct objects. **Check or fix:** Inspect seams during the entire camera move. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-023** Is an object hidden for so long that its return feels unmotivated? **Failure hypothesis:** Occlusion becomes an unexplained teleport. **Check or fix:** Retain an audible or visual continuity cue. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-024** Do atmospheric layers correctly exclude nearer objects? **Failure hypothesis:** Fog paints over the wrong depth range. **Check or fix:** Inspect depth masks against scene distances. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-025** Can depth be understood without relying solely on size? **Failure hypothesis:** Identical-size assets flatten the scene. **Check or fix:** Use overlap, ground contact and perspective cues together. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-026** Are depth transitions smooth enough for the intended medium? **Failure hypothesis:** Layer snapping produces unintended spatial jumps. **Check or fix:** Use controlled sorting boundaries or continuous geometry. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-027** Does the background show through a supposedly solid object? **Failure hypothesis:** Alpha or topology holes contradict solidity. **Check or fix:** Check object opacity and silhouette interiors. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-028** Are multiple viewpoints showing the same occluded state? **Failure hypothesis:** Views disagree about object visibility and position. **Check or fix:** Synchronize cameras to one scene-time sample. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-029** Does a label's leader line terminate on the visible object? **Failure hypothesis:** Occluded endpoints attach to the wrong surface. **Check or fix:** Reproject anchors and inspect final pixels. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-030** Is the occlusion itself doing useful storytelling? **Failure hypothesis:** Obscuration creates difficulty without payoff. **Check or fix:** Remove arbitrary blockers from the causal beat. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-031** Does the depth treatment remain coherent during a zoom? **Failure hypothesis:** Screen-space layers scale as incompatible distances. **Check or fix:** Compare scaling rates across near and far planes. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-004-032** Can a viewer tell an intentional hidden clue from missing artwork? **Failure hypothesis:** Unexplained gaps resemble rendering errors. **Check or fix:** Design a readable occluder and fair reveal. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

### D005 staging and blocking

- **AVQ-005-001** Is the character's immediate goal visible in the staging? **Failure hypothesis:** Movement has no readable target. **Check or fix:** Place goal and actor in a clear spatial relationship. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-002** Does the actor face the important event when it occurs? **Failure hypothesis:** Blocking hides attention and response. **Check or fix:** Align gaze and torso with the attended event. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-003** Are cause and consequence visible in the same beat? **Failure hypothesis:** Staging separates the action from its result. **Check or fix:** Reframe or add a motivated reaction cut. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-004** Does a supporting character accidentally steal the scene? **Failure hypothesis:** Secondary action is larger or earlier than the main action. **Check or fix:** Reduce its motion during the decisive beat. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-005** Can characters reach their props without implausible stretching? **Failure hypothesis:** Prop placement exceeds the rig's natural reach. **Check or fix:** Check reach envelopes before animation. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-006** Is a crucial exchange hidden behind another body? **Failure hypothesis:** Blocking conceals possession transfer. **Check or fix:** Open a clean sightline to the handoff. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-007** Does each character have a distinct action rather than generic motion? **Failure hypothesis:** Busy movement substitutes for intention. **Check or fix:** Assign one playable objective per beat. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-008** Are entrances timed to establish identity before interaction? **Failure hypothesis:** New actors arrive and act before recognition. **Check or fix:** Allow an orienting pose or entrance cue. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-009** Does the exit direction preserve the established geography? **Failure hypothesis:** A character leaves toward an impossible destination. **Check or fix:** Track screen and world directions together. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-010** Is the obstacle positioned where it can genuinely impede the goal? **Failure hypothesis:** Conflict is asserted without spatial support. **Check or fix:** Show the blocked route and available alternatives. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-011** Do characters queue in a visually traceable order? **Failure hypothesis:** Crowd staging implies multiple conflicting lines. **Check or fix:** Trace the queue path through turns and overlaps. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-012** Is a reaction staged where viewers are already looking? **Failure hypothesis:** Important response occurs outside the attention region. **Check or fix:** Move the reaction or guide attention before it. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-013** Does the hero turn away during the most expressive pose? **Failure hypothesis:** Blocking hides the emotional information. **Check or fix:** Use a readable three-quarter orientation. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-014** Are characters standing too close to read as separate agents? **Failure hypothesis:** Bodies merge into an ambiguous group. **Check or fix:** Create meaningful spacing or stagger depth. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-015** Does the staging support the intended power relationship? **Failure hypothesis:** Height and distance contradict the dramatic roles. **Check or fix:** Compare status cues with the scene's intention. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-016** Can the viewer see the object before it becomes important? **Failure hypothesis:** A prop appears only at the solution. **Check or fix:** Plant the prop naturally in an earlier composition. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-017** Does the solution require an unestablished route or opening? **Failure hypothesis:** Blocking invents space at the climax. **Check or fix:** Establish usable pathways before the payoff. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-018** Are pauses placed where bodies can actually balance? **Failure hypothesis:** A held pose would topple without support. **Check or fix:** Shift weight or add a justified contact point. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-019** Does simultaneous action overload the viewer's attention? **Failure hypothesis:** Several causal events compete within one interval. **Check or fix:** Sequence actions or establish a deliberate hierarchy. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-020** Are paired characters differentiated by behavior and position? **Failure hypothesis:** Symmetric blocking makes identities interchangeable. **Check or fix:** Give each a distinct stance and objective. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-021** Does the actor's path cross readable text at the wrong time? **Failure hypothesis:** Action occludes information during its reading interval. **Check or fix:** Separate path and text regions or retime them. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-022** Are the most important hands visible from the chosen angle? **Failure hypothesis:** Torso or costume hides the acting instrument. **Check or fix:** Adjust elbows, prop position or camera. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-023** Does the crowd leave enough room for the hero's performance? **Failure hypothesis:** Population fills the gesture envelope. **Check or fix:** Reserve a clear acting pocket. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-024** Is the destination introduced before a long traversal? **Failure hypothesis:** Travel feels arbitrary until the endpoint appears. **Check or fix:** Show a destination cue at departure. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-025** Does the blocking explain who initiates cooperation? **Failure hypothesis:** Joint action lacks a readable decision. **Check or fix:** Stage an initiating gesture and acknowledgment. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-026** Are background actors reacting to the same event time? **Failure hypothesis:** Crowd responses imply conflicting causes. **Check or fix:** Align reactions to the shared event clock. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-027** Does repeated staging make escalation feel unchanged? **Failure hypothesis:** New attempts look like copies of the first. **Check or fix:** Change spatial stakes or action strategy meaningfully. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-028** Is the final tableau readable as a resolved relationship? **Failure hypothesis:** Ending poses do not show what changed. **Check or fix:** Stage the new ownership, distance or shared goal. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-029** Can a viewer follow the hero through a crowd crossing? **Failure hypothesis:** Identifying features are hidden during a critical transition. **Check or fix:** Preserve a visible marker or isolate the path. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-030** Does the staging accidentally imply aggression or danger? **Failure hypothesis:** Friendly interaction uses threatening proximity or angles. **Check or fix:** Review body orientation and interpersonal distance. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-031** Are offscreen actions sufficiently established to be believable? **Failure hypothesis:** Plot depends on unseen unexplained activity. **Check or fix:** Provide a clear setup or bounded evidence insert. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-005-032** Would a single repositioning remove the need for explanatory dialogue? **Failure hypothesis:** Language compensates for unclear spatial relationships. **Check or fix:** Try a clearer blocking solution first. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** visual. **Stages:** storyboard, production, final-encode. **Style filters:** any.

### D006 contact and props
