- **AVQ-032-023** Can the previous approved version be restored after an unsuccessful edit or dependency upgrade? **Failure hypothesis:** The workflow overwrites the only working source and output. **Check or fix:** Verify versioned backups and perform a bounded restore test. **Severity:** high. **Modes:** machine. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-024** Are external URLs or cloud services required at render time documented with an offline fallback where feasible? **Failure hypothesis:** Network changes make a previously working local project fail. **Check or fix:** Test dependency availability and preserve authorized local inputs. **Severity:** high. **Modes:** machine. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-025** Does the package avoid including credentials, session tokens, or machine-specific secrets? **Failure hypothesis:** Reproducibility packaging leaks authentication material. **Check or fix:** Inspect configuration and archives for secrets without exposing their values. **Severity:** critical. **Modes:** machine. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-026** Are expected runtime, memory, storage, and temporary-file requirements recorded from an actual run? **Failure hypothesis:** Another machine cannot reproduce the output within available resources. **Check or fix:** Capture resource use and distinguish measurements from estimates. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** machine. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-027** Does automated cleanup preserve authoritative sources and only remove declared disposable outputs? **Failure hypothesis:** Broad deletion patterns destroy irreplaceable project material. **Check or fix:** Review cleanup scope and test it against a protected fixture tree. **Severity:** high. **Modes:** machine. **Stages:** production. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-028** Can a maintainer trace a delivered frame or sound back to its source revision and responsible edit? **Failure hypothesis:** Final output provenance is lost across transformations. **Check or fix:** Retain build manifests linking outputs, source commits, and asset versions. **Severity:** high. **Modes:** machine. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-029** Are known defects and untested routes documented so a future maintainer does not repeat disproven assumptions? **Failure hypothesis:** A clean handoff conceals unresolved problems and failed approaches. **Check or fix:** Record reproducible defects and distinguish untested alternatives from working routes. **Severity:** medium. **Modes:** machine. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-030** Does a format round trip preserve the specific hierarchy, animation, or editability promised to the recipient? **Failure hypothesis:** Export/import success is mistaken for preservation of native behavior. **Check or fix:** Round-trip a representative source and compare promised features explicitly. **Severity:** high. **Modes:** machine, visual. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-031** Are factual, rights, and dependency records linked to the assets they must be rechecked against when those assets change? **Failure hypothesis:** Maintenance updates bypass the reviews invalidated by the change. **Check or fix:** Define review triggers from the dependency graph and test one controlled replacement. **Severity:** high. **Modes:** machine. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

- **AVQ-032-032** Can a new maintainer complete one realistic revision using only the package and its instructions? **Failure hypothesis:** Documentation looks complete but omits practical steps needed to work. **Check or fix:** Run a bounded handoff exercise and repair observed gaps. **Severity:** high. **Modes:** machine, human-audience. **Stages:** production, delivery. **Style filters:** any.

## Appendix F Earlier artifact identities and evidence

The following selected artifacts at the 9 October cutoff were reconciled from local completion and validation receipts in the earlier handoff. They are examples and reusable source references, not a complete count of the entire Library. Version numbers are pinned observations; a future agent should resolve the latest version before editing. The hash is the exact SHA-256 recorded for the listed bytes. A save receipt does not establish publication, audience success, or Baltor intake.

### Progressive duck scale

The completed Large to Tiny episode is 56 seconds at 24 fps with 1,344 frames and an answer of seven. Its source records seven original Blender-rendered character cutouts placed on a photographic scene, continuous shared camera motion, and an independently designed puzzle image. The 720×1280 composition is upscaled to 1080×1920 for delivery. It is not real duck footage. The receipt records full final video/audio decode, sampled motion checks, geometric contact/bounds checks across 96 poses per duck, archive/hash checks, and reopen of three Blender files. Narration is absent. Human difficulty and engagement remain unmeasured.

### Creative motion decision board

The completed decision-board episode is 54 seconds with 1,296 frames and native 1280×720 composition. Its separate image is a deliberate decision-board artifact. The receipt records full decode, sampled motion, source import and exact poster reproduction, plus sampled aesthetic review. The encoder completed; a decoder-cleanup hang was handled after the final output existed, and the saved source bounds cleanup waits. This is a useful example of retaining a completed result while accurately recording a cleanup failure rather than declaring the whole job either perfect or lost.

### Floating to grounded comparison

The completed comparison is 48 seconds with 1,152 frames. It uses a 720×1280 composition upscaled to 1080×1920, with a separately composed 1600×1800 teaching poster. The source uses actual earlier and later revisions. The background crop also differs, so this is a multi-change design comparison rather than a controlled single-variable experiment. The receipt records full decode, 96 motion samples, source reopen, and exact poster replay. No audience preference or causal conversion result is established.

### Original duck meme and style studies

Everybody Act Natural is a 32-second original character-meme episode with 768 frames, original character animation, reaction poses, and composited contact shadows. Its native composition is 720×1280 and delivery is 1080×1920. The receipt records native Blender reopen and source archive reopen. It has no narration and no measured comedy or engagement result. A composited shadow is not a physical shadow-catcher pass.

Missing Key Magnifying Glass and Five Duck Minutes are separate 40-second style outputs with 960 decoded frames each and AAC audio. Their exact output hashes and full Library readback matches are recorded above. They share an editable-source package and do not add new native harness-capability credit merely because two finished videos exist.

### Brass key motion revision

The current pinned Curiosity Cabinet key output is version 2, with a separately saved drifting-light style variant. The associated source package is version 4. These later motion identities supersede the older static-search source for the purpose of assessing the requested camera-motion correction. A future agent should inspect the actual latest frames rather than assuming every historical README describes the newest video.
